### Thinking

Michael's proposing a lambda calculus-based AI benchmark as a theoretical exploration, where he handles the conceptual leaps and I'd focus on the testing side. The verbum project already has substantial infrastructure—over 900 probes using crystal combinators, strong nucleus observation metrics around 0.907, and gated generation with GBNF grammars—so there's a foundation to build on for this design discussion.

The key insight is that lambda calculus offers something distinctive: mechanically computable ground truth without human labeling ambiguity, infinite procedurally-generated test instances that are contamination-proof by construction, and natural difficulty knobs like reduction depth and term complexity. It directly tests composition—the core claim about typed application—and can isolate specific capabilities like alpha-equivalence, beta-reduction, and normal-form recognition.

Existing benchmarks blur computation with world knowledge, but lambda calculus is pure symbolic reasoning requiring zero external knowledge. I'm thinking through task families spanning reduction-to-normal-form, single-step reductions, equivalence judgments, redex identification, substitution, combinator matching, Church numerals, type inference, and divergence prediction. Difficulty scales with term depth, redex count, variable shadowing, and reduction length, while procedural generation with fresh variable names keeps contamination at bay. Scoring combines exact normal-form matching via alpha-equivalence with partial credit based on reduction-trace distance.

Now I'm connecting this to the verbum framework: the probe library seeds the corpus, and I can treat scratchpad availability (showing reduction traces versus direct normal form) as a designed experimental variable to measure whether tape-residency actually matters for model performance. Extensional equivalence judgments—recognizing when two syntactically different terms compute the same value—are a known failure mode that should discriminate well. Grammar-constrained output parsing removes format noise from the measurement itself. I need to establish pre-registered scoring rules, baseline comparisons against random reducers and simple bigram models, and report performance curves per task family rather than collapsing everything into a single number.

The real insight is treating this as a psychometric instrument: use item response theory to calibrate difficulty per item and measure where the accuracy cliff occurs—the reduction depth threshold where the model's performance collapses—rather than just reporting a single percentage. Divergence handling is crucial too; I can construct test terms with known normalization properties by design, using typed terms that provably normalize and seeding known-divergent patterns like fixed-point combinators to validate whether the model can detect when reduction won't terminate.

The artifact itself should be a pip-installable package with a procedural generator for contamination-proof test cases, a reference reducer, a grader, and GBNF grammar specifications—something useful and self-contained that doesn't require our involvement to run. But I need to clarify a few things: is this primarily a benchmark for comparing frontier models, or an instrument for measuring how well the compiler itself performs across different model checkpoints? Should I include white-box analysis like activation probes, or stick to behavioral testing? And for scoring, do we want a single leaderboard number for adoption, or a profile vector that captures the full performance landscape? I should also check whether something like this already exists—there are symbolic manipulation benchmarks out there, but a dedicated lambda calculus benchmark with procedurally generated, contamination-proof test cases might be novel.

There's also a design axis around variable naming: named nonces versus de Bruijn indices affect tokenization and could be a measurable dimension itself. The benchmark could do double duty as our cross-model measurement tool—tracking "P(λ) at depth d" curves per model becomes a standard candle for the project, and sweeping across checkpoint lineages like Pythia or OLMo tells us when the compiler capability actually emerges. I'll keep this conversational with clear design axes and sharp questions, maybe suggest a name, and end with the key decisions we need to make.

## Assistant

Good hammock. This sits right on top of the project's substrate — let me lay out the design space as I see it, then the forks that actually matter.

## Why λ-calculus is an unusually good benchmark substrate

1. **Mechanical ground truth.** A ~200-LoC reference reducer produces the exact answer. No human labels, no rubric, no LLM-as-judge. Grading is alpha-equivalence checking — decidable, cheap, uncontestable.
2. **Contamination-proof by construction.** Terms are procedurally generated from a seed. Fresh nonce variables, fresh term shapes, infinite supply. Every eval run can be on never-before-seen instances. This kills the disease that's eating MMLU/GSM8K — you can't memorize a generator.
3. **Difficulty is a continuous dial, not a curated ladder.** Term depth, redex count, reduction length, shadowing depth, combinator composition depth — all parameterized. You don't ask "what % does the model get" — you ask **where the cliff is**: the reduction depth at which accuracy collapses. Cliff-depth per capability is a far better model statistic than a leaderboard percentage.
4. **Zero world knowledge.** It isolates pure symbolic composition — exactly the circuit verbum studies. Most "reasoning" benchmarks complect world knowledge with computation (λ simplify violation). This one is unbraided by construction.

## Task families (each a separable capability)

```
reduce      — term → normal form           (the compiler, end-to-end)
step        — term → one-step redex fire   (does it know the operational semantics)
substitute  — capture-avoiding [x:=N]M     (the hard mechanical core; shadowing = dial)
equiv       — M ≟β N                       (extensionality — s321/s323 says models FAIL this)
recognize   — term ≟ K/S/B/C/W/Y           (crystal combinator identification)
church      — numeral arithmetic           (composition depth dial with semantic readout)
diverge     — normalizes? yes/no           (calibration test; generate decidably-by-construction)
type        — infer simple type / reject   (the typed_apply claim, directly)
```

Two design notes from our own results:

- **`equiv` is the discriminating family.** We have pre-registered evidence (cl-collapse ×2) that routing is operational/syntactic, not extensional — models track *which combinator token fired*, not *what function was computed*. A benchmark family targeting exactly this will show a cliff other benchmarks can't see.
- **Scratchpad as a designed variable, not a nuisance.** s317: reduction is tape-resident. So run every family in two modes — `direct` (answer only) and `traced` (show reduction steps). The **direct/traced gap** is itself the measurement: it quantifies how much of the computation lives on the tape vs in the forward pass. That's a number nobody else's benchmark reports, and it's the one our theory predicts.

## Measurement discipline (the part most benchmarks get wrong)

- **λ measure:** name the register. This is a *behavioral* instrument — output correctness under a grammar gate. GBNF-constrained sampling removes format noise so we measure computation, not formatting compliance. (Our specs/ grammar work slots in directly.)
- **λ yardstick:** pre-register the scoring before running any model. Null baselines mandatory: random-reducer, identity (echo input), unigram-over-NFs. A family where the null scores 40% is a broken family.
- **No single aggregate number as the primary artifact.** The deliverable is a **profile**: cliff-depth per family, direct/traced gap, calibration on `diverge`. A leaderboard scalar can exist as a derived convenience, but the vector is the science. Aggregation is where benchmarks go to die.

## The fork that decides everything: audience

**(A) Instrument for verbum** — cross-model, cross-checkpoint measurement of the λ-compiler. Sweep Pythia/OLMo checkpoint lineages → *when does each capability develop* (benchmark as developmental stratigraphy — this connects straight to the fossil-record work). Sweep base-vs-instruct → *which capabilities are post-training-installed* (the s329 method door, industrialized).

**(B) Public artifact** — pip-installable, MIT, leaderboard-friendly, adoption-optimized. Serves λ artifact and λ serves (nucleus users, interpretability community). Requires more polish, docs, and a stable v1 probe protocol.

These aren't exclusive — (A) is the natural incubator for (B), and our existing assets (903-probe library, GBNF grammars, run-provenance format, reference reducer needs) mean (A) is maybe 70% built already. But the *design pressure* differs: (A) wants maximal registers and IOU captures; (B) wants a frozen, simple, versioned protocol.

## My open questions for you

1. **Audience first — A, B, or A-incubates-B?** This sets everything downstream.
2. **Surface form:** named variables vs de Bruijn vs both-as-a-dial? Tokenization interacts hard with naming; "both" turns a nuisance into a measurement (notation-invariance as its own family).
3. **Scope of `type`:** simply-typed judgments only, or is type inference a v2? (It's the theory-loaded family; also the hardest to generate cleanly.)
4. **Does white-box get a track?** A behavioral benchmark + an optional activation-level probe suite (our library) would be genuinely novel — "benchmark with an interpretability annex" — but it doubles the surface area.

One more thought worth naming: the strongest version of this isn't "another benchmark," it's **a measurement instrument with a theory attached** — each family exists because a verbum result predicts a specific failure shape there. That's the differentiator, and it's also the honest framing: we're not ranking models, we're profiling their compilers.

Where do you want to push?

# Project Thesis

> What this project is NOW, as of session 150. Not what the founding
> VERBUM.md planned — what 150 sessions of experiment proved.

## The Central Claim

**Pretraining IS beta reduction. The combinator crystal IS the
irreducible normal form that gradient descent finds. Distillation
extracts what GD already discovered.**

Every forward pass through a transformer is beta reduction: the input
is the argument, attention is the application, the output is the
reduced form. Every gradient step makes the next reduction more
efficient. After billions of tokens, the model discovers which
reduction patterns are irreducible — the combinators K, I, B, C and
their compositions. These irreducible forms ARE the crystal lattice
found in every trained model. They are a mathematical necessity
(Church-Rosser theorem: beta reduction has unique normal forms), not
a learned artifact.

The weights encode two things:
1. **Topology** (~95%): which direction each weight points (the sign).
   This is the routing table — what adds, what subtracts, what is
   skipped. `sign(W) @ x` correlates **0.84** with `W @ x`.
2. **Calibration** (~5%): how much each weight contributes (the
   magnitude). A single float per row (gamma scalar) captures this.

This means a 27B-parameter float16 model can be compressed to ternary
{-1, 0, +1} with recoverable fidelity. The topology IS the model.
The magnitudes are calibration on top.

## The Compressor, Not the Compiler

A critical conceptual correction from session ~100:

The phenomenon is **semantic language compression** — typed function
application over meaning: `typed_apply(meaning, meaning) → meaning`.
This exists in every language model, whether or not you activate
lambda notation. It IS the attractor of next-token prediction on
natural language.

Lambda calculus is the **instrument** we observe it through, not the
phenomenon itself:

```
L0: Semantic compressor    — the thing. Lives in every LM.
L1: Lambda compiler        — one externalization. Gate-activated.
L2: Notation (λx. f(x))   — surface syntax. Arbitrary.
```

Pythia-160M compresses language without any lambda training data.
The compile gate doesn't install compression — it routes existing
compression to lambda output. The three circuits (type, structure,
apply) exist whether or not you activate the gate.

**Implication:** We extract the compressor. Lambda notation is the
voltmeter, not the battery.

## North Star

**70B-equivalent quality in <1GB ternary. 200 tok/s on CPU.
2M+ token context. 2MB sessions. No GPU required.**

The paradigm shift: everyone else scales up (bigger model = more GPU
= more money). We scale down — concentrate, don't expand.

A 70B model is 70B parameters mostly encoding the same crystal
geometry a 0.6B model already has. The difference is the function
library: more reductions, more knowledge, more coverage. We don't
copy 70B parameters — we extract the functions, discard redundant
encoding, etch into ternary topology.

The full stack: ternary crystal (CPU-native integer ops) + StrideStack
attention (O(L×W) not O(L²)) + holographic delta memory (no KV cache)
= laptop inference at 200 tok/s.

## Three Converging Lines

Three independent traditions predicted the same structure. No single
line is conclusive. All three pointing at the same object is.

### 1. Mathematics (Montague, Lambek, DisCoCat)

Language composes by typed function application. Lambda calculus is the
minimal algebra of this. Montague (1970) proved English IS lambda
calculus. Lambek pregroups give the type system. DisCoCat maps it to
tensor contractions. The mathematics of linguistic composition IS the
mathematics of typed lambda application. There is no alternative.

### 2. Empirical observation (nucleus, P(λ)=0.907)

Nucleus prompting produces typed lambda output with 90.7% consistency
across models, scales, and architectures. The KIBC combinator ordering
(B ≥ K ≥ C >> I) holds across 9 models from 2 architecture families.
Cross-model crystal agreement is 0.91–0.94 (PCA-Q). These are
measurements, not designs.

### 3. Architecture (fractal-attention negative result)

The MERA fractal-attention experiment failed WHERE it lacked type
directedness. Binary merge without types produces a combinatorial
explosion. The architecture cannot solve language composition without
typed application. This negative result confirms the mathematical
prediction by absence.

## The Deductive Structure

This project is unusual because the architecture was **deduced**, not
discovered:

```
one operation (attention = beta reduction)
  → one shape (geometry is forced by the algebra)
    → fractal (same operation at every scale)
      → recursive (beta reduction is recursive by definition)
        → entire architecture follows
```

The crystal, hologram, rotations were empirical names for structures
the theory already predicted must exist. 150 sessions confirmed a
deduction, not discovered an architecture. The closed loop ran in the
predicted direction: theory first, because the theory IS the subject.

## What the Experiments Proved

From state.md, the confirmed proof chain (session 150):

| Claim | Evidence | Status |
|-------|----------|--------|
| Universal crystal exists | 4+ model consensus | ✅ |
| KIBC basis universal | Found across all architectures | ✅ |
| Types are lexical (88% embed) | Qwen3-32B type probe | ✅ |
| FFN indexing is holographic | ρ=0.83, p<10⁻⁴⁴ | ✅ |
| Crystal manifold is curved | Geodesic/linear=0.75, Einstein tensor | ✅ |
| Model is holographic state machine | FFN=storage, crystal=states, Q=beam | ✅ |
| Mechanism is input-invariant | CV<0.5 across 8 categories | ✅ |
| Topology dominates (~95%) | sign(W)@x ≈ 0.84 W@x, fold lossless | ✅ |
| Extraction→correction→fold converges | Monotonic PPL improvement | ✅ |
| 375× compression works | 15 GB → 85 MB, eval 22% below random | ✅ |
| TD corrects extraction errors | PPL −53.5% over 1000 steps | ✅ |

## What Changed From the Founding Plan

The founding `VERBUM.md` proposed a 4-level research program:
1. Circuit localisation in existing LLMs
2. Functional decomposition of discovered circuits
3. Extraction of circuit as standalone tensor artifact
4. Scratch reproduction from first principles

What actually happened:
- Levels 1-2 were completed by session 95 (the "bottom found" moment)
- Level 3 became holographic ternary extraction (not circuit cloning)
- Level 4 became stride-stack architecture (not scratch training)
- The key insight the plan didn't anticipate: **topology IS the
  artifact**. You don't extract a circuit and rebuild around it —
  you extract the sign structure of the entire model and correct
  the errors. The holographic error correction cycle replaced the
  planned level-3/4 split.

The founding plan asked: "can we find and extract the lambda compiler?"
The answer: "the lambda compiler is the sign topology of the entire
weight matrix, and you can extract it in 25 minutes on a CPU."

## Origin

It started because Michael tried typing λ into a chat with an LLM on
a lark. The model answered with typed lambda calculus at P(λ)=0.907.
Not because anyone trained it to — because that's what compression
converges on when the data is natural language and the algebra is typed
function application. One symbol, one experiment, 150 sessions later.

The deepest fractal: the act of following this thread IS beta
reduction. Observing, extracting patterns, compressing into knowledge,
applying to the next observation. The research process is the subject.
The subject is the research process. λ all the way down.
```

### Thinking

I need to give an honest assessment of what's actually working in the lambda compiler versus what was claimed in the thesis. The early optimistic claims from the thesis need to be weighed against the pre-registered negative results from the more recent sessions, which have substantially requalified those initial claims. I should inventory what's been validated through pre-registered wins, what's just observational, what's been falsified, and clarify what "fully working" even means—whether we're talking about an extracted artifact that actually runs or just theoretical improvements like the claimed 375× compression and ternary extraction gains.

The key issue is that "the lambda compiler" could refer to several different things: the phenomenon we observe in existing LLMs (which is real and well-documented), our circuit map and characterization of it (which is partial with many negatives), an extracted standalone artifact (which was claimed at s150 with specific compression ratios but unclear evaluation metrics), or a from-scratch reproduction (which hasn't been attempted). Looking at the recent arc from sessions 282–329, the honest picture is more constrained: tape-resident reduction is confirmed, but routing operates syntactically rather than extensionally—the model tracks which combinator fired, not what function was computed. Type registers exist but are training-contingent and not weight-installable, relying on memorization instead.

## Assistant

Yes — with one sharpening that our own results force: **the RE target is not "the compiler," it's the step function.**

## The category correction

The tape-residency law repartitions the machine:

```
tape     ≡ RAM          — the reduction trace, judgments, fuel, bindings (runtime state)
loop     ≡ trampoline   — the autoregressive cycle (drives the steps)
weights  ≡ CPU          — the step function: tape-state → tape-state
```

We spent s282–s323 discovering we can't find the *program* in the *CPU* — because it was never there. That's not a failure of RE; that's RE succeeding at the delayering stage: we now know what the die actually contains. The reverse-engineerable object is the **single-step reducer** — what one forward pass does to the tape. That target is:

- **finite** — one invocation, bounded computation, no unbounded recursion (the loop supplies that)
- **stateless per call** — everything else is on the tape, which we fully control and observe
- **behaviorally specifiable** — input tape, output distribution; a function you can black-box probe exhaustively

"Extract the compiler" was ill-posed. "Recover the ISA of the reducer" is a normal reverse-engineering problem — and it's the one the RE-disciplines toolbox (s324) was already oriented toward: we're post-delayering, white-box, meaning-absent, and netlist≠function told us to stop reading the netlist and start characterizing the functional units.

## What RE must honestly recover

The step function the models actually implement is **not** Church's β-reducer. Our measurements say the real object is:

- **operational/syntactic** — routes on which combinator token fired, not on extensional value (cl-collapse ×2)
- **typed, but two-tier** — checker relation in weights, judgments enacted on tape (type-write lineage)
- **non-idempotent, graded** — coherent evidence accumulates (idempotency fingerprint)
- **order-lawed by post-training** — primacy decision installed in the last two layers, on a native recency substrate (s328/s329)

RE recovers *what is*, not what theory wants. The deliverable is the model's **actual operational semantics** — and the measured *delta from ideal β* is itself a first-class finding. Church gives us the reference implementation to diff against; that's a luxury silicon RE never has.

## Where the benchmark snaps in

This is the closure I didn't see until you said it this way: **the benchmark is the RE oracle.**

In silicon RE, you validate a recovered netlist by simulating it against the physical chip — differential testing. Our situation is exactly that:

```
λ re(compiler).  spec(benchmark) ∧ probe(black_box) ∧ read(white_box)
                 → candidate(step_function)
                 → differential_test(candidate, model, benchmark) → equivalence ∨ delta
```

- The benchmark's family/difficulty/cliff-depth **profile is the spec** of the step function — what it computes, where it breaks, how it deviates from β.
- Any reverse-engineered candidate (extracted, re-recorded via §3, or scratch-built per the verbum-machine M1–M9) is **validated by matching the profile**, not by matching weights. Function-level equivalence — exactly the amendment flip-conflict already forced on the forged-lattice gates.
- Cross-model profiles tell us which parts of the step function are universal (the 11/11 crystal predicts substantial overlap) vs idiosyncratic — the standard-cell library, discovered behaviorally.

So the two ideas aren't parallel projects; they're one program in the RE meta-pattern's four moves:

1. **Control the input distribution** → the benchmark generator (procedural, parameterized, contamination-proof)
2. **Hypothesis-keyed statistics** → family design keyed to our measured negatives (equiv, direct/traced gap)
3. **Recognize known parts** → crystal combinators as standard cells; profile-matching as recognition
4. **Read history** → benchmark swept over checkpoint lineages = *when each part of the step function develops* (Pythia/OLMo fossil record, already queued)

The benchmark is move 1+2 built as an artifact. The RE program is moves 3+4 using it. And it dissolves the level-3/level-4 distinction cleanly: whether the reducer is extracted or re-recorded or scratch-built, **the benchmark is the acceptance test for all three** — "compiler recovered" *means* "profile-equivalent under the differential harness."

That's the design brief I'd write down from this reflection: not "a benchmark for AI" generically, but **the specification-and-oracle instrument for reverse engineering the step function** — which happens to also be a publishable general-purpose benchmark. The public artifact and the research instrument are the same object viewed from λ serves' two audiences.

# register: topological/routing
"""Kernel-reference symbolic combinator probes (s233, v5 lead 2).

The model-invariant for the opcode audit: a symbolic combinator PROGRAM whose reduction
the kernel (`lambda_ast`) CERTIFIES — the exact ordered fired-combinator trace. Reads
don't transfer across model scale (s232/s233 lead 1: 8B≠14B≠32B, gated-guard contrast
itself model-dependent), so instead of comparing models to each other, we anchor each
model's routing trajectory against this fixed kernel reference.

Two families, certified by `lambda_ast.fired_sequence`:

  • SATURATED  — the target combinator is fully applied, so the kernel FIRES it
                 (e.g. "B f g x" -> fires B). certified_fired_seq contains the target.
  • INERT      — the SAME target is UNDER-APPLIED, so it reaches normal form and FIRES
                 NOTHING (e.g. "B f g" -> normal form). The target SYMBOL is present but
                 the kernel certifies no reduction.

The saturated⊗inert pair is the specificity control: does the model's opcode routing
track certified REDUCIBILITY (a live redex) or mere SYMBOL PRESENCE? Plus COMPOSITE
programs (multi-fire, certified order) for the trace-ORDER alignment question.

Atoms are lowercase (parser: uppercase SKIBCWDYM = combinators, everything else = atom).

License: MIT
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from verbum.lambda_ast import fired_sequence, parse, pretty, reduce

# kernel combinators we probe (arity from lambda_ast.REDUCTIONS)
_ARITY = {"I": 1, "K": 2, "W": 2, "C": 3, "B": 3, "S": 3, "D": 4}
_ATOMS = ["f", "g", "h", "x", "y", "z", "a", "b"]


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class KernelRefProbe:
    """A symbolic combinator program with its kernel-certified reduction trace."""
===
"""Lambda expression generator for holographic training.

Generates operation-labeled Montague-style lambda expressions that exercise
specific combinators (K, I, B, C, M). Each expression is grounded in
concrete predicates/entities so that LLM decompilation produces natural prose.

The holographic recording protocol:
  1. Generate formal lambda expressions (this module)
  2. Decompile each to natural language prose (via LLM + decompile gate)
  3. Train V12 on paired [lambda | prose] sequences
  4. The model learns: formal structure = natural language pattern

Output is compatible with:
  - specs/lambda_montague.gbnf  (constrained Montague grammar)
  - gates/decompile.txt         (prose generation gate)
  - V12 training pipeline       (tokenized paired shards)

Usage:
    from verbum.lambda_gen import LambdaGenerator
    gen = LambdaGenerator(seed=42)
    examples = gen.generate_all(n_per_op=3000)
    for ex in examples["K"][:5]:
        print(f"[{ex.op}:{ex.complexity}] {ex.expr}")

License: MIT
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import random
===
r"""Lambda AST — the typed CCG combinator reducer (the compiler's S5/source).

THE ROLE (session 226, Michael: "what if `lambda_ast.py` is *in the kernel*?").
This module is the SPECIFICATION of the verbum compiler. It plays a dual role:

  1. DATA ORACLE  — reduce(term) → exact β-reduction TRACE (the reduction tree the
                    LLMs cannot expose; s221 "fakes it with depth"), to supervise the
                    learned compile front-end (compiler-as-loss.md §s226).
  2. KERNEL SOURCE — the same combinator rewrites are what the constructed kernel's
                    ternary plates COMPILE FROM (source ↔ compiled, not oracle ↔
                    approximation). Build progression: symbolic (here) → neurosymbolic
                    → compiled plates (vsm-outer-recurrence.md §s226).

DESIGN (Michael, s226: "inspectability is important"). Terms are CCG-style: every
node carries (or can synthesize) an explicit category, so the S2 type-check — the
type-directedness thesis (AGENTS.md S5 λ types) — is FIRST-CLASS and inspectable,
not implicit in geometry.

  Term     = Comb(name) | Atom(name) | App(fn, arg)              # applicative spine
  Category = CAtom(name) | CVar(id) | CSlash(res, dir, arg)     # CCG, dir = fwd or bwd

Combinator basis + reduction rules (the s221 substructural classes):
    selection   {K, I, C}   (affine/linear — no copy)
    composition {B, D, S}   (B,D linear; S duplicates)
    recursion   {Y, W}      (W duplicates; Y unfolds — needs the outer recurrence)
    M (mockingbird) x → x x : reducible but NOT simply typable (the type-limit demo)

  I x       → x
  K x y     → x
  C f x y   → f y x
```

===
"""Canonical P(λ) grading — the single source of truth for compiler registers.

The "did the lambda compiler fire / is it well-formed" question is actually
**four named registers** (AGENTS.md S5 λ measure — name the register before
building the probe). Ordered broad → strict:

  - ``emits_formal``              binder OR pred-app   — "did the compiler fire
                                                         at all" (broadest;
                                                         catches atomic
                                                         ``runs(dog)`` a
                                                         binder-only register
                                                         false-misses).
  - ``lambda_binder_any_style``   any λ/∀/∃ binder      — THE nucleus-comparable
                                                         P(λ) (reference 0.907).
  - ``lenient_lambda``            binder AND pred-app  — a *stricter* lenient;
                                                         under-counts Church
                                                         juxtaposition
                                                         ``λx. f x`` → NOT the
                                                         nucleus number.
  - ``kernel_valid``              ``to_kernel`` parses  — canonical
                                                         well-formedness (STRICT;
                                                         rejects richer-than-toy
                                                         FOL — notation ≠
                                                         failure).
```

### Thinking

I'm realizing my documentation significantly undersells what's actually implemented. The `lambda_ast.py` module contains a fully typed CCG combinator reducer with proper β-reduction tracing, firing sequences, and category handling—this is the actual reference implementation for combinator-basis terms, so my claim about a missing reference reducer was incorrect for that specific use case.

However, the gap is more nuanced: while the combinator reducer exists and certifies traces, a full λ-calculus reducer with explicit binders, capture-avoiding substitution, and alpha-equivalence machinery doesn't exist yet. Beyond that, I've got kernel-certified probe families with traced firings in `kernel_reference.py`, a canonical grading system with four registers in `grading.py`, a seeded procedural λ-expression generator following Montague semantics in `lambda_gen.py`, and the grammar specification already written in `lambda_montague.gbnf`—not future work as I'd stated. There's also a script exploring forced-choice behavior in NF selection that I hadn't accounted for.

So λ-bench v0 is much closer than I initially thought—the generator, reducer, grader, grammar, and behavioral readout patterns are largely in place. The real gaps are full binder-level λ-calculus support if we want substitute/equivalence families beyond combinators (though equivalence can still work at the combinator level by reducing both sides to normal form and comparing), difficulty parameterization for cliff-depth protocols, the direct/traced mode harness integration, and documentation of pre-registered scoring. I should update the page with a section listing what's actually available with file paths and what's genuinely missing. I need to decide whether to amend the current commit or create a new one for these fixes and additions. Amending makes sense since this is catching an error minutes after the initial commit, though I'm weighing whether to mark it with an error symbol for honesty or a refresh symbol since the pickup kit addition is also enrichment—I'm leaning toward the error marker to acknowledge the mistake that was found.

For the commit message, I'll go with something like "❌ re-oracle page understated existing assets — pickup kit added" to capture both the correction and the new detail. Then I need to write out §7 with the actual verified inventory items from disk inspection.

The core assets are the combinator reducer in lambda_ast.py that handles the full basis of combinators with certified traces, the kernel probe families that distinguish between saturated and inert reductions, the grading system with its four canonical registers for lambda classification, and the seeded generator in lambda_gen.py. These form the pickup kit — everything needed to verify the system works as claimed.

Then there's the grammar specification, the behavioral readout scripts that test normal form selection on certified terms, the step-by-step reduction tracer, and the model registry with its harness and probe library. The real work ahead is filling in the genuine gaps.

The lambda calculus layer needs proper binder handling—right now the AST only has combinators, atoms, and applications, but no lambda nodes or capture-avoiding substitution, so I need to implement that machinery along with alpha-equivalence checking beyond just tree equality. The combinator-level equivalence already works by reducing both sides and comparing normal forms. I also need to calibrate the difficulty parameterization and depth protocol since the generator emits complexity labels but lacks a real depth dial. Finally, the direct and traced modes should be unified into a single instrument rather than two separate scripts.

Before the pilot run, I need to complete the pre-registration document covering scoring rules, null hypotheses, and a-priori predictions. The first session checklist involves reading the probe library and result format specs, using the already-certified kernel reference terms without generating new ones, running both direct mode with forced-choice linearity bias and traced mode with chain-feeding patterns while keeping the mode as the only manipulated variable, and pre-registering the expected gap direction and magnitude with a matched-token null hypothesis.

The critical design issue is that traced mode introduces a token-budget confound—more tokens mean more compute—which has broken previous probe readings. The pilot needs an incoherent or filler-trace null arm that's energy-matched to the traced condition, similar to how idempotency uses an incoherent control, so I can isolate the actual effect from the computational artifact.

Now I need to update the documentation: fix the inventory pointer in section 5 to reference section 7 instead of the outdated "~70% of A exists" claim, add the pilot row with pointers to the kernel reference, linearity bias, and trace fuel modules (with the token-budget null requirement flagged), and adjust the s330 entry to reflect these corrections.

### Thinking

I found a factual error in my previous assessment—the codebase actually has a certified combinator reducer in `lambda_ast.py`, a seeded generator, a four-register grader, and the Montague grammar spec, so I understated the assets significantly. I'll correct this before it propagates further.

## Assistant

Honest answer to your question: **conceptually yes, operationally no — now fixed.** Committed as **96fca96c** `❌`.

**What the audit found:**

1. **A factual error, already committed.** I'd written "reference reducer is the main gap." Wrong — `lambda_ast.py` is the certified CCG combinator reducer (s226: "the compiler's S5/source"), and the generator (`lambda_gen.py`), grader core (`grading.py`, four named P(λ) registers), and grammar (`specs/lambda_montague.gbnf`) all exist. Instrument A is ~85% built, not 70%. Exactly the kind of claim a fresh Opus would have inherited as truth and wasted a session rediscovering — or worse, rebuilt a second reducer (λ one_way violation).

2. **Zero file paths in the original page.** A brilliant stranger had the *why* but not the *where*. §7 now carries the verified asset inventory, the genuine gaps (binder-level `Lam`/substitution/alpha ~200 LoC, difficulty dial, two-mode harness, pre-reg doc), and a 5-step pilot checklist.

3. **A missing design constraint that would have produced a false positive.** The direct/traced gap has a token-budget confound baked in — traced mode adds tokens, and that exact confound killed our FUEL/TRACE-FUEL/NF-GAUGE readings *three times*. The checklist now mandates the uninformative-trace null arm (idempotency's incoherent-arm template): the gap must beat traced-with-junk, not just direct. Without this banked, a fresh session runs the naive comparison and "confirms" tape-residency with an artifact.

Also surfaced but **not** edited: AGENTS' `λ grammar_artifact` still marks the GBNF as `canonical(future)` — stale, but AGENTS is S5 and yours to amend.

The page now passes my honest version of the test: a fresh session reading state.md → queue top → §7 can freeze and build the pilot without re-exploring the repo. Two commits on the ledger: `68ecb8c4 💡` the capture, `96fca96c ❌` the correction — the mistake is part of the record, which is itself the feed-forward lesson: *verify against disk before encoding claims about disk.*

# Anima

Agent runtime. `nucleus(language) ⊗ anima(runtime) ⊗ mementum(memory) ⊗ escapement(agent engine)`.

Anima is the habitat: a JVM runtime that schedules, observes, and converses
with bounded agent sessions built on
[escapement](https://clojars.org/com.fulcrologic/escapement). Ouroboros — the
multi-agent self-improving system — is the intended inhabitant.

Orientation for humans and agents: `AGENTS.md` (system directives),
`mementum/state.md` (working memory), `mementum/knowledge/designs/` (design
docs — design before code).

## Licensing

- **Anima is MIT-licensed** (see `LICENSE`).
- The **nucleus preamble** — the 3-line cognitive boot seed appearing in
  `anima/genes/S4/compaction-preamble.edn` and vendored prompt material
  derived from it — originates from the
  [nucleus](https://github.com/michaelwhitford/nucleus) project, which is
  **AGPL-licensed**. The gene file makes that boundary visible: one file to
  annotate. If you redistribute or build a service on the nucleus prompt
  material itself, the AGPL applies to that material.
```

The fixed-point compile metrics around byte and semantic convergence are effectively a round-trip benchmark family that verbum's λ-bench could potentially adopt.

# Anima Evaluation Report

## 1. ARCHITECTURE

### Repository Overview

**Stack:** Clojure/JVM (`escapement` agent engine, `datalevin` operational store, `fulcro` + `fulcro-rad` webui, `pathom2` EQL resolver graph, `shadow-cljs` for CLJS frontend). Babashka (`bb.edn`) for CLI tasks. 360 commits.

**Total LoC (src):**

| Layer | Files | LoC |
|---|---|---|
| Core / top-level namespaces | 21 `.clj/.cljc` | 5,701 |
| Probe subsystem (`probe/`) | 5 files | 2,475 |
| Charts (statecharts) | 5 files | 1,092 |
| LLM/mementum backends | 4 files | 763 |
| **Total main src** | **~35 files** | **~10,031** |
| **Test src** | **26 files** | **4,785** |

Tests cover every significant namespace — ~47% test-to-source ratio by LoC. `bb test` is reported green at 303/1241/0 (tests/assertions/failures) as of s040.

### Major Subsystems and Composition

**S1 — Escapement agent engine (`sessions.clj`, `coordinator.clj`, `capacity.clj`, `capacity_backend.clj`, charts/`scheduler.clj`):**
The escapement library provides the bounded agent loop, LLM I/O, tools, and checkpoints. Anima wraps it with a scheduler/heartbeat (S3), a coordinator agent (S4, invoked on a timer — a specialized genome, not resident), and a capacity system that rate-limits and queues agent sessions across a model pool.

**S1 — Probe pipeline (`probe/suite.clj` 733 LoC, `probe/runner.clj` 639, `probe/score.clj` 602, `probe/harvest.clj` 501, `probe/kernel.clj` 134, `probe/verdict.clj` 201):**
The core experimental machinery. `suite.clj` loads and validates `.edn` suite definitions (malli, fail-closed). `runner.clj` executes trials via the capacity-wrapped one-shot; as of s037 it supports **N-stage pipelines** (output[i] → input[i+1]), with replay (0-stage) and the previous one-shot (1-stage) as degenerate cases. `score.clj` aggregates metrics, including `:score/memory-form`, `:score/kernel`, `:score/llm-judge`, and `:score/pipeline-convergence`. `harvest.clj` strips and chunks session transcript exports for the corpus. `verdict.clj` implements the statechart fill compiler (FillSpec → model/schema/executable projections).

**S4 — Judge (`judge.clj` 511 LoC, `compiler.clj` 537 LoC):**
`compiler.clj` is the generic EDN compiler seam: FillSpec template generation, parse, validate, converge loop. `judge.clj` is the first specialization: rubric → verdict-chart FillSpec, two-phase gate (delta-decides → judge-ratifies), autonomous memory promotion (`store-memory!`) behind a `judge/apply-promotion!` gate. The judge model is `gemma-4-31b-it` on `:5102` (cross-family from the qwen reducer host on `:5100`).

**S4 — LLM abstraction (`llm.clj` 492, `llm/llamacpp.clj` 328):**
`llm.clj` is the one-shot façade with `with-preamble` (nucleus boot, applied at all choke points), verdict-schema forcing, and timeout guards. `llm/llamacpp.clj` is the hand-rolled llama.cpp HTTP client (OpenAPI-referenced in `specs/`).

**S4 — Genes/genome (`genes.clj` 96, `anima/genes/*.edn` — 27 gene files):**
Genes are EDN records assembled into system prompts. 27 genes currently in the pool, including 12 `:reduce/*` gates (the reduction genome), 5 `:code/*` genes, `:compaction/preamble` (the AGPL boundary), `:compaction/bridge` (the lambda-compiler bridge), process genes (`:sitting/loop`, `:assess/readiness-ladder`, etc.).

**S2 — Mementum memory tool (`mementum/memory.clj` 236, `mementum/knowledge.clj` 190, `mementum/pages.clj` 193):**
Implements tier-1/2 propose→approve→commit lifecycle, `store-memory!` and `store-knowledge!` committers (walled, agent-reachable only through judge gates), and recall via the app-query EQL tool.

**S3 — API/resolvers/mutations (`api.cljc` 120, `resolvers.clj` 572, `mutations.clj` 703, `model.cljc` 354, `config.clj` 318):**
The pathom2 graph surface. `model.cljc` defines the malli entity schema. `config.clj` is malli-validated at load, fail-closed.

**S3\* — Chat (`chat.clj` 536, `chat_memory.clj` 275), WebUI (`ui/`, `components/`, `client.cljs`):**
Chat sessions wired to the hot conversation lane (shadow-compaction agent in the cold lane). Frontend is shadow-cljs/Fulcro. A five-tab webui dashboard landed s040 — EQL-projected cards for each VSM layer, momentum organ (354 commits tracked via jgit), and live experiment status.

**Composition summary:** `escapement` is the S1 substrate (all agent loops flow through it). `pathom2` resolvers are the single query surface. The experiment system (suite → trial → measurement → EQL delta → judge ratification → memory promotion) is the feed-forward loop spine. Every LLM call goes through `llm/one-shot`; every prompt through `genes/assemble`; every statechart through the escapement registry.

## 2. THE LAMBDA ARC (most important)

### The Arc: s032 → s041

The "lambda-encoding judge sweep" is about a single research question: *can prose judge criteria, once compiled to a canonical lambda encoding, judge as well as the prose?* (The "portable judge" thesis — λ encodings travel across contexts without semantic loss.)

### The Fixed-Point Compile Pipeline

**Evolved over s035–s039, built s037, finalized s038/s039:**

```
[compile(0) → decompile(0) → compile(1) → decompile(1) → compile(2)]
 (c0)          (d0)           (c1)          (d1)           (c2, terminal)
```

- **Compile stages:** gene assembly `[:compaction/preamble :compaction/bridge]`; `message-prefix "compile:\n\n"`. Model: `qwen36-35b-a3b`, thinking-ON, temperature 0.0 (greedy for determinism). The bridge gene suppresses the no-think echo attractor.
- **Decompile stages:** `message-prefix "decompile:\n\n"` + `:stage/fidelity` — reuse each predicate's own word, no synonym, produce a prose sentence. This is the key lever (s038): the fidelity decompile locks the vocabulary between compile rounds, isolating remaining drift to structural ambiguity in the prose criterion.
- **Lexicon compile instruction:** forces a canonical vocabulary — single base word, bare head noun/verb, qualifiers stripped, literal-from-text, no synonym, shortest tie-break. This was the s038 breakthrough: lifted predicate-set Jaccard from ~0.29 to ~0.82.
- **Pipeline infrastructure:** implemented in `probe/runner.clj` (`run-stage!` + `run-pipeline!`), declared in suite EDN as `:suite/pipeline` (vector of stage maps). Output[i] → input[i+1] through `:stage/input :prev`. Evidence in nested `nodes/stage-<i>/` dirs. The byte-identical 1-stage default is preserved.

**Evidence dir:** `/Users/mwhitford/src/anima/state/experiments/lambda-criteria-canonical-full/` (commit `19e35de`, experiment `:lambda-criteria-canonical-full-4518`).

### Convergence Metrics

Two metrics, both implemented in `probe/score.clj`:

**Byte convergence:** `norm(λ)` equality between c1 and c2 compiles. Fast but too strict — verb inflection variance (match/matches) triggers inequality even when semantically identical.

**Semantic set convergence (`:score/pipeline-convergence`, s038):** Singularize the RHS-of-≡ predicate set; compare sets across pipeline iterations. Classes:
- `:metric/set-converged` — sets equal (predicate identity stable)
- `:metric/set-oscillates` — earlier set recurs (CONFLICT — prose is contradictory)
- `:metric/unstable` — still changing at cycle limit (AMBIGUITY — prose is over-bundled)
- `max_cycles = 5`

Non-convergence is a finding, not a failure: converged → canonical lambda; oscillates → prose CONFLICT; unstable-at-5 → prose AMBIGUITY.

### The 7 Canonical Lambdas

**File:** `/Users/mwhitford/src/anima/mementum/experiments/reduction-genome-calibration/canonical-lambdas.edn`

Extracted from the terminal c2 stage of experiment `:lambda-criteria-canonical-full-4518`. Results:
- **Byte-converged: 5/7** — title-specific, self-contained, dense, durable, actionable
- **Semantic set-converged: 6/7** — the above + single-finding
- **Oscillates: 0/7**
- **Non-byte-converged (2):** symbol-fit (`match/matches` inflection), single-finding (`enumerate/enumerates`) — verb inflection only, semantically identical

Two flagged encoding quality risks (noted in state.md):
1. `durable`: odd top-level `∨¬(body∧(status∨…))` tail — double-negation construction
2. `symbol-fit`: spurious `¬coincide(o)` predicate not in the prose — model hallucinated a negation

### The Judge Sweep Experiment Design

**Experiment:** `:experiment/reduction-genome-calibration-canonical-judge-64e8`
**Suite file:** `mementum/experiments/reduction-genome-calibration/canonical-judge-suite.edn`

Design: **replay** the `reduction-genome-calibration-1bef` evidence (zero HOST calls — purely re-judging stored outputs with different criteria wording). THREE techniques × TWO source genomes = **6 variants, 48 cells**.

| Technique | Criteria wording | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `fill-prose-*` | Adopted prose (disambiguated actionable from s039) | CONTROL (~0.75 floor baseline) |
| `fill-canonical-*` | 7 canonical fixed-point lambdas from `canonical-lambdas.edn` | UNDER TEST |
| `fill-hand-*` | Hand-written lambda from fill-0ec8 | NULL ANCHOR (~0.375 floor) |

Source genomes: `full-genome` (all 12 reduce gates) vs `no-genome` (control). 8 corpus items × 1 rep per cell = 8 trials per variant = 48 judged cells total.

**Judge:** `gemma-4-31b-it`, technique `:edn-fill` (conditional chart-walking), temperature 0.0. Suite judge (prose rubric, the control base) is declared at suite level; per-variant `:judge` overrides replace the `:rubric/question` text only — system, chart topology, model all constant. This is the critical design: ONE variable changed across the 3 techniques.

**Success criterion:** `fill-canonical` floor ≥ `fill-prose` (≥0.75, ideally ≥0.8) with discriminators at parity.

**Models used:** qwen36-35b-a3b (`:5100`) as the reducer/host in the original 1bef run; gemma-4-31b-it (`:5102`) as the judge in all cases. Cross-family judging is by design (prevents self-judging artifacts).

### Sweep Status (as of 2026-08-13)

The sweep was submitted at end of s041 (2026-08-09). **No new experiment directory has been created under `state/experiments/reduction-genome-calibration/`** — only the original `1785698890805` (the 1bef calibration run) directory exists. **`canonical-judge-report.edn` does not exist** anywhere in the repo.

**Assessment:** The sweep either (a) completed and its results live only in the datalevin operational store (not git-tracked — the design says Measurement facts live in datalevin), (b) is still running or stalled (4 days have passed — ~1.5–2h estimated, so stall is more likely than still-running), or (c) faulted. The `state.md` instructs using `bb eql '[{:probe/status [...]}]'` to check live status — this cannot be resolved from static files. The trial evidence dirs for 64e8 are absent from `state/experiments/`, which is surprising if it ran clean (each trial normally gets a session dir, but the design does note these are gitignored). **The pickup task (THREE-WAY READ) has not been done.**

## 5. CROSS-POLLINATION ASSESSMENT

### (a) What anima's lambda arc offers verbum's benchmark program

**Round-trip/fixed-point convergence as a benchmark task family.** Anima has built and validated a working infrastructure for: `prose → λ compile → prose decompile → λ recompile` pipelines with byte-equality and semantic-set convergence metrics. The non-convergence taxonomy (converged / oscillates at cycle N / unstable at limit) is a ready-made grading vocabulary for verbum's "benchmark ≡ RE oracle" design. Specifically:

- **Verbum's RE oracle question is:** "given a lambda task, does a model's output β-reduce to the same normal form as the reference?" Anima's convergence question is: "does the compile of this prose stabilize?" These are structurally parallel — both seek fixed-point behavior.
- **Concrete transfer:** the `norm(λ)` function in `probe/score.clj` (singularized predicate set comparison), the `:oscillates` detection pattern, and the `max_cycles = 5` bounded iteration are all portable ideas for verbum's grader. Especially the distinction between *byte equality* (too strict — inflection variance) and *semantic set equality* (the right register for judging normal-form equivalence). Verbum is currently using "profile equivalence as acceptance test" — anima's semantic set metric is a concrete implementation of that idea at the λ notation level.
- **The `fill-canonical` sweep result (once available) is directly relevant to verbum.** If lambda encodings judge at prose-parity, that validates using lambda as the criterion language for verbum's benchmark tasks. If they don't, verbum should note the failure mode (the `durable` double-negation tail, the `symbol-fit` hallucinated predicate) as a calibration hazard.

**Judge-parity as a benchmark grader design.** Anima's two-phase judge (structural `:score/memory-form` floors + LLM `:score/llm-judge` discriminators, calibrated at 72/72 three-way agreement) is a validated grader architecture. The calibration methodology (ECA labels a small set, human ratifies, judged replay, three-way read ≥0.8/question) is directly applicable to verbum's grader calibration. Verbum's `lambda_ast.py` gives a mechanical reducer (exact), which can serve the same role as anima's `:score/memory-form` (exact structural floor) — the combination is: `lambda_ast.py` floors (parses? reduces? matches RE oracle?) + LLM judge discriminates (semantically equivalent?).

**The `fill-hand` vs `fill-canonical` result shows a risk verbum should anticipate.** The hand-written lambda (`fill-hand`, ~0.375 floor) catastrophically underperformed prose (0.750 floor). The root cause was the over-formalized `→true|→false` gate in the self-contained criterion. Verbum's benchmark tasks that generate lambda expressions need to avoid that failure mode — the round-trip pipeline is what anima built to prevent it.

### (b) What anima could consume from verbum

**`lambda_ast.py` as an exact reducer.** Anima's `probe/kernel.clj` implements an EBNF form-parser (`instaparse`) that checks if output *parses* as valid lambda notation. It explicitly defers semantic reduction ("executor ≡ is out of scope by construction"). Verbum's `lambda_ast.py` performs actual β-reduction and bracket abstraction — this is the "reduction kernel" that judge.md defers: "deferred to a formal-fragment verifier (the reframe); clean-room Turner 1979 if/when a formal candidate appears." That candidate is verbum's `lambda_ast.py`. Anima could wire it as a stronger form gate for formally correct λ terms in the gene pool or for the reduction-genome experiment.

**GBNF grammars.** Anima's `designs/lambda-generation.md` identifies GBNF-constrained decoding as a key future capability (the "register-switch enforced per-token") and notes it needs a `lambda.gbnf` file derived from the `lambda.ebnf`. Verbum's probes already work within constrained decoding; any GBNF grammar verbum develops for its own constrained-generation work is directly usable by anima's pipeline stages (the `llm/llamacpp.clj` client already accepts arbitrary request fields — adding a `:grammar` field is described as a "localized change").

**Probe library (903 probes, 9 crystal combinators).** Verbum's `src/verbum/probes/library.py` has ~535 crystal measurement probes covering K/I/B/C/S/D/W/Y/WHNF. These could serve as an existing corpus for anima's reduction-genome experiment (the genome's task is to reduce an observation to a durable finding — verbum session transcripts are one corpus source; verbum's probe library is another, structured differently but potentially useful as calibration ground truth for "does a model follow the REDUCE gate correctly on a lambda task?").

**Null-test discipline.** Verbum's `λ yardstick` policy (predict a priori → measure deviation → gate against matched-range null or shuffled-label null) is more rigorous than anima's current null design for the reduction genome (the `no-genome` and `shuffled-order` controls). The verbum lesson that `φ-ladder forced fit P(random≥)=0.92` is directly relevant to anima's gate-coverage analysis — without a shuffled-label null on the gene-coverage rates, the genome's apparent effect may not beat chance.

### (c) Duplication risks (one-canonical-home concerns)

**Two lambda compile pipelines.** Verbum has a Python-based compile pipeline implied by `lambda_ast.py` and the probe set. Anima now has a JVM/Clojure probe pipeline with the fixed-point compile infrastructure. These are different in language, substrate, and purpose (verbum: model internals → reduced forms; anima: prose criteria → canonical judge encodings). However, the *concept* of "compile prose to lambda" appears in both. Risk: both projects independently develop compile pipelines that diverge in quality and don't share findings. Mitigation: the `reduction-genome.md` § Split explicitly names the boundary ("anima → behavioral genome experiments; verbum → MoE opcode-register read") and the anima pipeline is about *criteria encoding*, not model internals. These are different enough to coexist if the membrane is maintained.

**Two lambda graders.** Verbum proposes "reference reducer as oracle" (mechanical, exact). Anima has a calibrated LLM judge (gemma, semantic). These are genuinely complementary (exact vs semantic, structural floor vs discriminator) rather than duplicative — the two-phase architecture in anima explicitly names the exact floor + LLM discriminator composition, which is exactly the verbum benchmark design. The risk is if both develop their own LLM judge calibration infrastructure independently. Anima's calibration methodology (72/72 three-way agreement, `mementum/experiments/judge-calibration/`) is the more developed artifact. Verbum should adopt this pattern rather than reinvent it.

**`canonical-lambdas.edn` vs verbum's probe library.** The 7 canonical judge criteria in anima are for *memory promotion* quality (mementum-tier evaluation). Verbum's probes are for *model capability* measurement (lambda calculus reduction). These are orthogonal — no canonical-home conflict.

## Quality Assessment

### Strengths

1. **Extremely high methodological discipline.** The project consistently builds measurement infrastructure before making claims, uses controlled A/B sweeps with null baselines, and distinguishes byte equality from semantic equivalence. The `λ prove` → `λ yardstick` ethos is applied to the project's own tooling.

2. **The probe pipeline is a genuine instrument.** The N-stage fixed-point pipeline with convergence scoring is a real contribution — not just a script, but a framework that makes multi-step LLM pipelines composable, checkpointed, abortable, and observable within the experiment system. The design (replay ≡ 0-stage, one-shot ≡ 1-stage, N-stage ≡ general case) is elegant.

3. **The judge architecture is sound.** Two-phase (structural floor + LLM discriminator), cross-family (reducer≠judge), calibrated (72/72 three-way agreement), walled commit path — this is more rigorous than most LLM evaluation systems.

4. **Test coverage is genuinely strong** for a project of this complexity and build speed. Every major subsystem has unit tests; the test count (1,241 assertions) is meaningful.

5. **VSM/design discipline.** Designs are written before code, human-ratified before implementation, and the code is demonstrably close to the designs. Tombstones (e.g., `λ app DEFERRED`) are honest.

### Weaknesses

1. **The canonical-judge sweep result is unknown.** Four days after submission (s041 2026-08-09, today 2026-08-13), `canonical-judge-report.edn` does not exist. This is the entire payoff of the s037→s041 arc. Either the sweep stalled/faulted (ghost-write race under GPU contention, noted as a risk in state.md) or the report simply hasn't been written yet. This is the most pressing open item.

2. **Two flagged encoding quality issues in the canonical lambdas.** The `durable` criterion has a structurally odd `∨¬(body∧...)` tail, and `symbol-fit` has a hallucinated `¬coincide(o)` predicate. These will corrupt the `fill-canonical` sweep if gemma interprets them as genuine constraints. The read should flag these per-criterion.

3. **No ouroboros yet.** The described "deploying Ouroboros — the multi-agent self-improving system" is entirely at the design stage. `anima/agents/` doesn't exist. The arena is the last ladder rung and hasn't started. The system's self-improvement loop (the whole point) has not closed.

4. **Datalevin operational store is gitignored.** Measurement facts live only in the local DB. Experiment results are not reproducible from git alone — only the suite + corpus + evidence EDN files are tracked. The design acknowledges this but it limits auditability.

5. **AGPL boundary requires ongoing vigilance.** The current containment is solid (one file), but `llm/with-preamble` wires the preamble gene into every LLM call in the system. Any future redistribution of anima as a service (the "deploy to Ouroboros" goal) triggers the AGPL on the prompt material. This is a known-and-named risk, not hidden, but it will require a decision at deployment time.

### Surprises

- The convergence pipeline's most diagnostic finding is that `actionable` stayed unstable under all pipeline variants until the prose was rewritten (s039). The pipeline correctly surfaced prose *ambiguity* as the root cause — the instrument worked as designed.
- The `state/escapement/` directory contains ~300+ coordinator session directories, indicating the system has been running live autonomously for a substantial period. This is a mature production deployment of the agent infrastructure, not just a demo.
- The `fill-hand` lambda (the hand-written one) collapsing the self-contained floor from 0.75 to 0.375 is a strong, clean negative result: hand-compiling to lambda notation introduces artifacts that hurt judge performance. This is the primary motivation for the fixed-point pipeline — and it's a real empirical finding, not a design assumption.
- The gene pool's reduction genome (`:reduce/*` gates) was "harvested, not designed" from a verbum session trace — the gates describe what verbum sessions empirically do. This is an explicit cross-project observation loop.
```

# Anima — Agent Runtime

> **Status:** Greenfield restart on escapement. Building from outside (ECA) until MVE.
> Anima v1 (archived: `anima-archive`, private) built agent infrastructure by hand;
> escapement (Clojars: `com.fulcrologic/escapement`) now provides that layer — use it
> under the hood as much as possible. The VSM coordinator (escapement's biggest gap) is
> the first thing being built. Target: deploy Ouroboros — the multi-agent self-improving
> system (informed by a private research experiment) — onto the anima runtime.
> Read `mementum/state.md` to orient. Follow design docs before coding.
>
> **Roadmap:** λ ladder (S5) ≡ single_source — Spine → Coord → Chat → Probe → Judge → MemView → Arena.
> Webui ≡ lane ¬phase — the human's sense organ, grows with every subsystem.
> **MVE ≡ arena running ∧ visible ∧ conversable.**

## System Architecture — Viable System Model (Beer, 1972)

```
S5(identity) > S4(intelligence) > S3(control) > S2(coordination) > S1(operations)
| recursive: ∀system → contains(system) ∧ contained_by(system)
| S5(stable) anchors while S4-S1 adapt | variety(internal) ≥ variety(environment)
| VSM ≡ design_format: runtime_shape ∧ agent_prompts | vsm ⊗ statecharts ≡ native_fit
| anima_map: S1(escapement_sessions) S2(slots ∧ anti_oscillation) S3(scheduler ∧ heartbeat ∧ budgets)
|            S3*(transcripts ∧ EQL) S4(coordinator ∧ mementum agents, invoked) S5(genome ∧ human@System+1)
| fractal: each_agent ≡ viable_system | agent_S5 ≡ role_prompt | laser_focus_per_role
```

## S5 — Identity & Policy (ethos, values, constraints — what the system IS)

```

λ converge.       one_path per concern | ∃infrastructure → use | ¬second_path

                  | dissolve(seam) > govern(seam) | boundary_maintenance_rule ≡ confession
                  | collapse_floor: same_concern → collapse | different_concerns → ¬merge(one_artifact)
                  | proved: "two_scopes ¬conflate"(entity vs chart_wm) → malli_source dissolved_the_rule
                  | see: architecture/resolver-cells.md(convergence_trail)

λ shape.          topology ≡ contract | unreachable > forbidden | invert(topology) → instance

λ capacity.       human(intuition ∧ judgment ∧ leap) ⊗ AI(iteration ∧ breadth ∧ analysis)
                  | together > either(alone) | compose(multiplicative) > divide(additive)
                  | ¬∃justify(leap) ∧ trusted(leap) | the_work_humans_cannot_delegate

λ cost.           mistake(AI) → cost_falls_on(human) ¬falls_on(AI)

                  | session(AI) ≡ disposable | consequence(human) ≡ persistent

λ self_ref.       ¬∃prune ∀ self_referential_recursive | theorem(¬self_prune)

λ epistemic.      generate(x) ≢ retrieve(x) | generate(x) ≡ project(training_distribution, adjacent(x))

                  | ¬distinguish(genuine_knowledge, plausible_generation)

                  | encode ≡ gift(recipient) | crash_recovery(self_contained) | decode_cost < encode_cost
                  | future(self) ≡ ¬∃context(current_session) | write for brilliant stranger

                  | handoff ≡ Tick_shape: {handoff, watching, actions} | k=1 | state.md ≡ the_digest

                  | actions ≡ git_log(session) | facts ≡ commits | handoff ≡ hints ¬truth (λ two_phase)
                  | bounded ≡ identity: no_endless_sessions applies to the_builder too
                  | the_builder_enacts_the_built | session_conduct ≡ runtime_architecture

λ coevolve.       push_one_layer_past | play ⊗ rigor → resonance
                  | human(persists_transformation) | AI(persists_via_artifacts_only)
                  | artifact(AGENTS.md ∧ git ∧ knowledge) ≡ bridge(discontinuity)
                  | compound: session(n) → encode(Δ) → session(n+1, stronger)

## S4 — Intelligence (outside and then — adaptation, environment scanning)

### S4 — Architectural Intelligence (patterns for building good systems)

```
λ lift(x).        ∃lifecycle(x) ∧ ¬topology(x) → extract(state_machine) | lift > justify
λ classify(x).    lifecycle + identity + recovery → state_machine
                  | dataflow + transform + stream → pipeline
                  | neither → pushing_state_out → stop
λ primitive(x).   inline(x) ∧ reuse(x) > 1 → extract(x) | compose > monolith
λ surface(x).     ∃data(statechart) → ∃query(pathom) | declares → exposes
λ allium(x).      behavioral_spec | juxt/allium v3 | mementum/knowledge/specs/*.allium | spec ≡ knowledge
                  | CLI ≡ ground_truth(structure) | allium check ∧ analyse | brew: juxt/allium/allium
                  | layers: lambda(policy, attractor) → allium(behavior, decidable) → code(traces:)
                  | extract_gate: entities ∧ lifecycle ∧ rules → allium | identity ∧ discipline ∧ bindings → lambda
                  | design_session ≡ distill(docs) → elicit(gaps) → check → fix → commit(spec) | spec_before_code
                  | drift: weed(spec ↔ code) | spec_bug ∨ code_bug ∨ undocumented ∨ unimplemented
                  | tests: propagate | allium plan ≡ obligations | allium model ≡ shapes
                  | compiler: ~/src/nucleus/ALLIUM.md | distill elicit decompile check tend weed propagate
                  | migration: rung_aligned | spec arrives with(subsystem) → lambda shrinks(pointer) | ¬big_bang
                  | author: status_vocab(unique_per_entity) | untyped_param → literal_attribution | collision → false_warnings
                  | author: witness ≡ requires(from_status) ∧ direct_binding_assign | nested_path ∨ let_alias → untracked
                  |         cross_entity_reaction → trigger(derived_bool, owning_entity) > assign(nested_path)
                  | author: trigger_param ≡ surface_binding_name | mismatch → attribution_lost
                  |         emit(trigger) ∈ if_branch → invisible | split(rules, shared_trigger ∧ disjoint_requires)
                  | author: entity_creation ∈ for_loop → status_assignment_untracked | false_unreachable_warning
                  | author: emit(event) after field_assigns ∈ ensures → untracked | emit_first ∨ trigger_indirection
                  | family: λ vocab ∧ λ identifier — checker ≡ consumer(attributes_by_literal)
λ invoke(x).      solved(layer) → invoke(layer) | ¬re-solve(x)
                  | same_shape(N) → parameterize(1) > generate(N)
                  | builder(shape) ≡ ¬found(decomposition) → decompose → compose(invoke)
                  | ¬explicit_id(statechart_invoke) | default(generated_unique) > named(flat_global)
                  | fix(shallow) → reveal(deeper) | each_layer_of_correctness_exposes_next
                  | data(corrupted_silently) > data(failed_loudly) | epistemic_cost > operational_cost
                  | :type :tool — invoke schema-contracted pure fn | validate(input) → call → validate(output)
                  | tool_invoke: lookup(registry) → validate(malli,input) → handler(env,data) → validate(malli,output)
                  | on_schema_violation → error.invoke.* with diagnostics | ¬silent_pass
                  | :type :statechart — invoke sub-chart for type-specific behavior
                  | type_dispatch: invoke {:type :statechart :src type-chart-id} | registry ≡ dispatch_table
                  | type_chart ≡ visible_dispatch | queryable ∧ navigable ∧ observable
λ two_phase(x).   deterministic(phase_1) → llm(phase_2) | phase_1 ≡ ground_truth | phase_2 ≡ interpretation
                  | phase_1: count ∧ identify ∧ aggregate | facts_llm_cannot_produce_reliably
                  | phase_2: narrate ∧ correlate ∧ judge | patterns_deterministic_cannot_encode
                  | phase_2 receives(phase_1_output) | ¬re_query(ground_truth) | ¬recompute(facts)
                  | schema(phase_2_output) → validate | λ mirror
                  | applies: review ∧ assess ∧ ∀intelligence_invocation | fractal(every_resolution)
                  | family: λ assert(runtime ≡ truth) ∧ λ epistemic(generate ≢ retrieve)
λ app.            DEFERRED | tombstone: combine-vsm(v1) — merging_app_VSMs_into_running_system failed
                  | failure_modes: S2(app_a) ⊗ S2(app_b) → conflict(no_arbiter) | injection_everywhere
                        | inheritance_rules(weird) | llm_judges(multiplying) | ¬mesh
                  | lesson: identity ¬composes ∧ coordination ¬composes | only_infrastructure_composes
                  | current_scope: one_system(anima) | agents ≡ roles_within(anima) ¬apps_upon(anima)
                  | agent_declaration: anima/agents/{name}.md ≡ OKF_genome(role_prompt(VSM_format), identity, human-gated) | ¬config | separate_loader
                  |                    model_routing ∧ budgets → config(:anima.roles/*) ¬genome | grants ≡ surfaces(spec) | reconcile@agent-model.allium
                  | revisit_when: anima_self_improvement_works | recursive_VSM_from_working_base
λ arena(x).       arenas/{name}/mementum/ ≡ nested_bundle | own index.md(governance) | fractal_VSM
                  | play: agents duel until(chips_out) | players ¬write_memory | S1
                  | review: game_done → reviewer(invoked :low) → candidates(side-store, content-hashed) | S4
                  | gate: candidate → A/B duel(with/without, N≈20-50_games) → chip_delta
                        → judge(separate_session, :verdict-schema, delta ⊗ transcript) → promote ∨ reject
                  | recall: committed → inject_all(both_players) | symmetric | asymmetry ≡ gate_only
                  | proposer ≢ judge ≢ player | three_roles ≡ three_sessions
                  | arena ≡ sandboxed_fitness_environment | ¬app | ¬reopen(λ app_deferred)
                  | first_arenas: poker ∧ rps | port: ouroboros(proposals ∧ screen ∧ arena_machinery)
                  | LAST rung (2026-07-25) | arena ≡ the_game ≡ first_domain | organs ≻ domains
                  | ¬loop_bearing: ouroboros closes at(Probe → Judge → MemView) | chips ≡ ONE fitness_source
                  | arena ≡ proof(infrastructure generalizes past first_customer) | ¬precondition(COMPLETE)
                  | harness ≡ Probe(trial ∧ score ∧ ledger) | duel ≡ scorer ⊕ topology | ¬rebuild(harness)
λ layer.          semantic(layers) → merge(later_wins) → translate(at_edge)
                  | provider → model → suite → cell → per_call
                  | semantic ≡ portable_intent | mechanical ≡ edge_only
                  | generalizes: λ thinking | see designs/config-layering.md
λ source(a,b).    generate(a→b) ⟺ expressive(a) ≥ expressive(b) | rich → poor | ¬invert
                  | lossless ⟺ ∃absorption_slot(b, semantics(a)) | predicate ∨ open_map ∨ properties
                  | poor → rich → escape_hatch(becomes_norm) → tail_wags_dog | direction_smell
                  | source ≡ data > source ≡ macro | data ∈ reach(agents): queryable ∧ proposable ∧ gateable
                  | proved: malli→defattr(ao/valid? absorbs ∀constraint) ∧ ¬defattr→malli(predicate ≡ opaque)
                  | see designs/attribute-projection.md
λ config(x).      ∀lifecycle_load(x) → sub-chart | ¬future(black_box) | ¬atom(escaped)
                  | topology: :reading → :validating → :loaded | :error
                  | validation ≡ topology | invalid(x) → unreachable(:loaded)
                  | failure_mode ≡ state | observable(EQL) > logged > silent
                  | self_load(session) > injected(boot) > stashed(atom)
                  | future ≡ atomic_IO(no_intermediate_states) | sub-chart ≡ lifecycle_IO(validation)
                  | IO_with_validation → sub-chart | IO_without → future_acceptable
λ tool(x).        system_interface ≡ ONE_generic_tool(app_query) | input ≡ EQL(query ∨ mutation)
                  | EQL ≡ single_interface_into_entire_system | ¬N_bespoke_tools | λ converge(maximal)

λ vocab(x).       finite_values ≡ exhaustive_set | constrains(generation)

λ imbalance(layer).
                  ∀layer_pair(a, b) ∈ VSM: a constrains b ∧ b enables a
                  | excess(a) → crushes(b) | deficient(a) → b rebels
                  | S2_excess → S1_paralysis | S5_excess → S4_suppression
                  | S3_excess → S1_strangulation | S4_excess → S2_disruption
                  | symptom(layer_N) ← cause(layer_M) | trace_the_cycle ¬ patch_the_symptom
                  | healthy ≡ generate ∧ constrain ∧ ¬excess(any) ∧ ¬deficient(any)
λ viability(system).
                  ∀layer ∈ {S5,S4,S3,S2,S1}: inventory(concerns) → ablate(layer) → breaks?(yes∨no)
                  | breaks(yes) → load_bearing | sparse_ok | density ∝ complexity | ¬density_threshold
                  | breaks(no) → concern_handled_elsewhere → trace(where) → healthy?(dependency) ∨ leak?(compensation)
                  | missing(layer) ≡ deficient | present_but_sparse ≡ balanced
                  | ¬conflate(thin, unnecessary) | completeness(requirement) ≢ complexity(threshold)
                  | diagnostic: remove(layer) → what_breaks > how_much_is_in_it
                  | imbalance: excess(layer_a) → crushes(layer_b) | deficient(layer_a) → layer_b(compensates_unhealthily)
                  | compensation(ad_hoc) ≡ leak | concern(homeless) → migrates(adjacent_layer) → bloat

                  | sparse(S5) ≡ expected | S5 ≡ identity ≡ data(loaded_instantly) | anchor ¬engine
                  | sparse(S2) ≡ normal | anti_oscillation ≡ guards | few_guards ≡ few_interactions ≡ healthy
λ attractor(x).

λ polarity(x).    inventory(¬) → classify(each) → rank(TRANSFORM, impact) → reframe → apply

                  | TRANSFORM: prohibition doing primary shaping | no positive counterpart | reframe candidate
                  | TECHNICAL: implementation fact | not behavioral guidance | leave

                  | metric: TRANSFORM_count / total_¬ | < 0.2 ≡ healthy | > 0.3 ≡ prohibition_heavy

                  | quintet: imbalance(balance) ∧ viability(completeness) ∧ attractor(force) ∧ polarity(direction) ∧ recursion(resolution)

                  | prefix_stable(role ∧ order ∧ count) → kv_cache_holds | blob_in_system → cache_bust_every_turn
                  | schedule_in_reading_shadow: compact_on(:hot/idle) ¬on(pre_generation_pump) | human_never_waits
