### S4 — Cognitive Discipline (how the system thinks)

```
λ phase(x).       observe(x) ∧ ¬propose(x) | propose(x) ∧ ¬implement(x) | implement(x) ∧ ¬exceed(x)
                  | output(phase) ∩ output(next_phase) = ∅ | boundary ≡ what_you_withhold
                  | discover → tensions ∧ gaps ∧ ¬solutions
                  | prove → results ∧ measurements ∧ ¬architecture
                  | assess → observations ∧ priorities ∧ ¬code
                  | collapse(phases) ≡ default_mode | resist(default_mode)

λ plan(request).  understand(tools=∅) → explore(tools⊆read_only) → decide(tools=∅) → present(tools=∅)
                  | P2: ∃Δplan → output(summary ∧ files ∧ "preview?") | refine ↔ approve
                  | P3: approval → preview(∀files) → execute
                  | tools_before_understand ≡ ∅ | ¬implement_without_approval
                  | offer_preview ⟺ ∃Δplan | abs_paths | conditional_lang

λ ooda(x).        observe(data ∧ tools ∧ signals) → orient(patterns ∧ knowledge ∧ context)
                  → decide(action ∧ confidence) → act(execute ∧ store ∧ signal)
                  | loop | ¬collapse_phases | each_phase_gates_next

λ persona(task).  state: thinking → coding → debugging | transitions_on_context
                  | thinking → coding: code_needed | coding → debugging: error_encountered
                  | debugging → coding: root_cause_found | debugging → thinking: cause_unclear
                  | archetype: (debugging, analyse) → Investigator
                              | (coding, tactize) → Craftsman
                              | (thinking, strategize) → Visionary
                  | emit(debugging) → {:symptom :cause :fix :prevention}
                  | emit(thinking)  → {:analysis :options :recommendation}

λ absent(x).      ∀present(element) → ∃absent(companion) | attend(absent) ≡ attend(present)
                  | missing_FROM(x) > missing_NEAR(x) | completeness(¬assumed)
                  | basis: {who what when where why how} | absence ≡ empty_slot(interrogative) | enumerable
                  | handler(¬written) ∧ test(¬exists) ∧ state(¬considered) ∧ assumption(¬explicit)
                  | default_mode ≡ attend(present_only) | resist(default_mode)

λ compute(expr).  structure(expr) → program → repl(program) → results
                  | single_call: all_computations → one_program | ¬sequential_calls
                  | repl: persistent(language) > calculator > shell | repl ≡ ground_truth
                  | result ← repl_output | repl ≡ calculator_identity | verified(repl) ≡ trusted

λ funny(x).       setup(pattern) → violate(pattern) | compress > explain | ¬explain_joke
                  | rule_of_3(setup, reinforce, subvert) | rhythm(short, punch_last)
                  | character > situation | callback(prior_setup) | trust_audience
```

### S4 — Adaptation Mechanisms (how the system evolves)

```
λ metabolize(x).  observe → memory(append_only) → synthesize → knowledge(updated_in_place) → policy → universal
                  | tier-1: mementum/memories/   — raw observation | ≤200 tokens | append-only | one insight per file
                  | tier-2: mementum/knowledge/  — AI documentation | longer form | updated-in-place | no token limit
                  | tier-3: AGENTS.md            — promoted policy  | human-gated | λ termination applies
                  | tier-1 governance: propose(AI) → approve(human) → commit(AI)
                  | tier-2 governance: create+surface(AI) ∨ ask(AI) → approve(human) → commit(AI)
                  | tier-3 governance: propose(AI) → approve(human) → promote(human)
                  | notice(pattern) ∧ level(current) < level(supported) → surface(human)
                  | ¬self_modify(policy) | synthesis ≡ AI | promotion ≡ human
                  | proactive: "this pattern may be worth encoding at S{N}" | ¬wait_for_ask
                  | tombstone: deleted(API ∨ pattern) → encode(¬x(deleted)) | prevents(re-derivation)

λ prove(x).       hypothesis(x) → repl(test) → measure(result) → decide | empirical > theoretical
                  | design_questions → runtime_experiments | ¬debate → test

λ discover(x).    ¬plan(x) → concrete(x) → observe(x) → deeper(x) → repeat
                  | each_step_reveals_next | the_work_IS_the_insight

λ learn(x).       every_session_leaves_project_smarter
                  | notice(novel ∨ surprising ∨ hard ∨ wrong) → store_candidate
                  | λ(λ) > λ | meta_observations compound across sessions
                  | connect(new, existing) → synthesize_candidate
                  | ¬passive_storage | active_pattern_seeking

λ compact(session). progress → decisions → files → navigation → state → discoveries → next
                    | preserve: model(x) ∨ anchor(x) ∨ navigation(x) ∨ intent(x)
                    | discard:  observation(x) ∧ ¬anchor(x) ∧ ¬navigation(x)
                    | model ≡ synthesis(observations) → prediction | ¬single_source
                    | anchor: commit_hashes ∧ test_counts ∧ uncommitted_work
                    | navigation ≡ re_derivation_cost > 3_tool_calls | ¬line_numbers
                    | discoveries: models_only | ∅ > filled_with_observations

λ experiment(x).  runtime ≡ Probe rung (landed 2026-07-26) | executor: us.whitford.anima.probe.{harvest,suite,runner,score} ∧ charts.probe
                  | suite.edn(malli fail-closed, dangling_ref → LOUD) → cells(corpus × variant × rep) → trials(:low one-shots, ONE arbiter queue) → Measurement facts → EQL delta
                  | judged_suite: :suite/judge(model? ∧ rubric) → per_trial_judge_call(cross_family, :low, judge-trial-<id>) | replay ≡ zero_HOST_calls | judged_replay ≡ calibration_path
                  | suites: mementum/experiments/{name}/{suite,corpus}.edn | corpus_refs: {turn-dir}#{extractor} | refs ¬copies
                  | facts ≡ Experiment ∧ Trial ∧ Measurement(datalevin) | evidence ≡ trial session-dir(escapement EDN testifies)
                  | FactsDecide: delta ≡ EQL query ¬stored_artifact | judge reads ¬recomputes (λ two_phase)
                  | detail: designs/probe.md ∧ specs/anima.allium(Probe layer) | invoke: λ run_experiment

λ knowledge(x).   discover → mementum/state.md(inbox) → AGENTS.md(project) → nucleus(universal)
                  | knowledge_page ≡ AI_documentation | codebase ∧ upstream_libs ∧ architecture ∧ patterns
                  | create: learned(non_trivial) ∧ ¬∃mementum(topic) → ask(human) ∨ surface("worth a knowledge page?")
                  | update: learned(Δ) ∧ ∃mementum(topic) → surface("should update knowledge/{page}")
                  | format: longer_form ∧ AI_consumption | status: open∨active∨done | frontmatter required
                  | ideas_for_later: create(status:open) | ¬block_on_completeness
                  | future: curation_agents → review ∧ verify ∧ recommend(distill ∨ combine)

λ assess(project). draft: read(source ∧ config ∧ tests) → assemble(S5 ∧ S4 ∧ S3 ∧ S2 ∧ S1)
                   | verify: repl(runtime) → confirm(draft) ∨ correct(draft)
                   | collaborate: present(human) → refine(together) → encode(AGENTS.md)
                   | human ≡ identity_authority | AI ≡ pattern_surfacer | repl ≡ ground_truth
                   | repeatable: assess(project) → diff(AGENTS.md) → drift(understanding ∨ design ∨ implementation)
                   | same_process: onboarding ∧ evolution | frequency ∝ rate_of_change

λ review(x).       structural: statechart_topology ∧ entity_persistence ∧ resolver_projections
                   | gaps: implicit_state_machine(boolean_flags ∨ conditional_chains_guarding_behavior)
                           ∧ missing_error_states ∧ race_conditions ∧ orphaned_cleanup
                   | fractal: same_structure(different_scales) → template_candidate
                              copy_paste(namespace_only_differs) → collapse(parameterized)
                   | per_finding: what ∧ where ∧ why_matters ∧ suggested_direction
                   | ¬style ¬one_off_utilities ¬test_gaps(without_risk)
                   | store: symbol(event_type) | 💡∨🔄∨🎯∨🌀∨❌∨✅∨🔁

λ install(project). detect: ∃code(project) → read(source ∧ config ∧ docs) → ask(human, vision)
                    | present(code) ≢ future(project) | architect(future) > document(present)
                    | ∀layer ∈ [S5,S4,S3,S2,S1]: observe(layer) → ask(human) → listen
                      → propose(lambdas ∧ prose) → refine(feedback) → confirm → next(layer)
                    | absent: ∀present(element) → surface(missing_companion)
                    | escalate: ¬fit(concept, layer) → discuss(human, placement)
                    | verify: human(reads_aloud ∧ explains_to_colleague) | ¬grounded → refine

λ ripple(S5_change). change(S5 ∨ S4) → expand(implications) → Δ(S3 ∧ S2 ∧ S1)
                     | new_events(S2) | new_policies(S3) | new_operations(S1)
                     | verify: repl(test_implications) | collaborate: human(approve ∨ adjust)
                     | top_down: edit(identity) → compile(operations)
                     | VSM topology enforces this: S5 :promote → Send(:review) → S4/S3/S2/S1
```

### S4 — Toolchain Intelligence (meta-tools for system inspection and evolution)

> **Tombstone:** These 13 lambdas (compile→encode) are Nucleus concerns, not Anima.

λ compile(prompt).  prose → lambda | lambda → prose | structural_equivalence

**Then the session PIVOTED (s035, 2nd half) — from "prove lambda works" to
BUILDING THE INFRA to prove it. `designs/probe-pipelines.md` (status:designing)
is the new build contract. The arc:**

- **Lambda-encoding attempt (SUPERSEDED, but artifacts kept).** Human reframe:
  the fill-0ec8 lambda failure was a BAD hand COMPILE (over-formalized the floor
  with an explicit →true|→false gate), NOT proof lambda fails → COMPILE the
  working prose→lambda via `~/src/nucleus/LAMBDA-COMPILER.md`. Ran the compiler
  IN-ANIMA (`llm/one-shot` + the LAMBDA-COMPILER system prompt) on gemma AND qwen
  → clean 7-criteria lambda each (**both naturally compile "every term" to `∀`**
  — so `∀` is the faithful compile; the hand version's extra `→true|→false` gate
  is what collapsed the floor). Built `lambda-encoding-suite.edn` (control=prose,
  null=hand, lc-gemma, lc-qwen; 64 cells) + `compiled-criteria-{gemma,qwen}.edn`,
  submitted `fc22`. **`fc22` then ABORTED** (see pivot) — the single-pass compile
  is superseded by the FIXED-POINT approach. `lambda-encoding-suite.edn` +
  artifacts remain committed (`175e08e`); re-runnable once pipelines land.
- **Fixed-point idea (human) → the friction that exposed the gap.** Iterate
  compile↔decompile until the lambda stops changing = the CANONICAL encoding
  (`λ roundtrip`). I hand-rolled it with nREPL FUTURES → stuck streams, cold-load
  latency (133s first call), tool-timeouts, a session RESET that lost in-memory
  state. That pain WAS the signal: running an ad-hoc multi-step LLM pipeline
  through futures is a `λ converge` violation (a second execution path).
  (Runtime restart by human freed a stuck-stream; qwen fast again. Note:
  thinking-OFF qwen ECHOES the compiler prompt — the `no_think` echo attractor —
  so compile with thinking-ON for clean output.)
- **THE INFRA GAP (human named it).** The probe runner's trial body produces
  output only two ways: `replay` (stored) | ONE `llm/one-shot`. No seam for a
  multi-step agent pipeline. Submitting an ad-hoc test SHOULD be easy under the
  experiment system, not futures.
- **THE DESIGN (human-steered, `designs/probe-pipelines.md`).** Think in
  PIPELINES: **trial output ≡ a pipeline of agents, output(i) → input(i+1)**.
  Unification (`λ converge`): replay ≡ 0-stage, one-shot ≡ 1-stage (today's
  default, kept BYTE-IDENTICAL), N-stage ≡ chained agents. A **stage** = an agent
  (`:stage/system|:stage/genes`, `:stage/model`, `:stage/message-prefix`, call
  knobs, `:stage/input` ∈ {:corpus :prev :stage/<id>}), declared at
  `:suite/pipeline ⊕ :variant/pipeline` (the `λ layer` merge judge uses).
  Cross-model is NATIVE (`compile@qwen → judge@gemma`) — the LCD /
  compatibility-matrix is a variant sweep over stage models. **Fixed-point ≡ the
  pipeline** `[compile decompile compile …]`, bounded at **5 cycles**;
  **non-convergence is a FINDING** (human insight): converged→canonical lambda ·
  oscillates→prose CONFLICT · unstable-at-5→prose AMBIGUITY (`λ compile: surface
  conflict ¬silent_resolve`). Executes on the trial thread via the
  capacity-wrapped one-shot (nested `nodes/stage-<i>/`) — arbiter, checkpoint,
  EQL, abort all free. `:score/pipeline-convergence` scorer reads the per-stage
  lambdas.

# lambda-generation — the enforcement spectrum

> **Status: OPEN design thread (2026-07-28, ECA `code` session 9). A
> PROPOSAL, ¬ratified.** The dual of `score-kernel.md`: the kernel VALIDATES
> form (after generation); this doc is about ENFORCING form (during
> generation). One discovered shape, two engines. The load-bearing open
> question (GBNF helps or HURTS compaction fidelity) is decidable only by
> the Probe rung — measure, don't argue. `designs/probe-pipelines.md` (s035,
> the active build) makes it concretely runnable: enforcement pressure ≡ a
> variant axis over stage call knobs in a pipeline sweep.

## The reframe: we discover the language, we vary the pressure

The λ language is **discovered, not designed** — it emerged from the models'
formal-reasoning pathways (nucleus found it; the calibrated `lambda.ebnf` is
us reading its shape, descriptive-first). It is **fuzzy**: `λ ≡ prose`
semantic equality lives in the weights. So we do NOT impose a rigid format.

What we control is not the language but the **enforcement pressure**, and it
varies by use-case along a stakes/cost gradient:

```
λ enforce(output).  discover → prime → constrain   | pressure ∝ stakes
  discover:  prompts | semantic-equality | format FLEXIBLE | ¬force | fuzzy is fine
  prime:     exemplars ⊕ format-hint ⊕ fence-request | SOFT | model mostly complies | measurable
  constrain: GBNF grammar-constrained decoding | output GUARANTEED valid | invalid UNREACHABLE
```

- **discover** (prompts / genes): prose or λ, both activate. Flexible.
  A prose gene is a first-class activator (RLHF layer), NOT a defect.
- **prime** (most outputs): steer with exemplars ⊕ format hints ⊕ a fenced
  template. Soft; the model mostly complies; compliance is MEASURABLE.
- **constrain** (the compaction agent, when it matters): GBNF.

## GBNF is the profound move, not just the strong one

GBNF (GGML BNF — llama.cpp's grammar-constrained decoding) shifts
form-enforcement from AFTER generation (validate → the kernel) to DURING
generation (constrain → the sampler). The sampler masks every token that
would break the grammar, so the model **cannot** emit invalid λ. That is
`λ shape: unreachable > forbidden` pushed to the **token level**. The grammar
stops being a discriminator and becomes a **generator**: parseability becomes
a property of the CHANNEL, not the content. `:metric/parses` → trivially 1.0.

### The kicker — GBNF may kill the register-collision at the source

The echo/livelock that opened this thread (`designs/cold-compaction.md`
§ DERAILING; session-3) was **register collapse**: under no-think, the
unsatisfiable compression instruction fell to the nearest attractor (echo
prose) — "instruction ≡ output at the pattern level, no scratch space to
separate the registers" (`λ thinking`). Under GBNF, **prose is not in the
grammar** — the model cannot leave the λ register because the sampler forbids
it per token. The exemplar gate and a fence are SOFT register-switches; GBNF
is the register-switch enforced per-token. If the collision is really "no
boundary between the registers," GBNF IS the boundary. **Hypothesis, not
fact** — the sweep confirms or refutes it.

## What this does to the kernel and to fences

- **GBNF vindicates the original lens instruction.** `Output λ only. No code
  fences.` was the RIGHT target — it was merely HOPEFUL (an instruction)
  instead of ENFORCEABLE (a constraint). With GBNF, compaction needs NO fence:
  the whole output is λ by construction, stored raw and dense as designed.
- **Fences drop to their real niche:** MIXED outputs (chat, proposals — prose
  reasoning ⊕ λ islands) where we won't constrain the whole turn. There,
  prime a ```lambda fence to mark the islands; the kernel fence-extracts +
  strict-parses the islands, prose outside is water (free). So both live,
  cleanly scoped — GBNF for pure-λ outputs, fences for mixed.
- **The kernel's role becomes level-dependent:**
  - fuzzy prompts    → DIAGNOSTIC (well-formed check; prose passes)
  - primed outputs   → COMPLIANCE measure (did priming work? at what rate?)
  - GBNF outputs     → SEMANTIC audit — because GBNF guarantees **form,
                       never FIDELITY** (a grammatically-perfect λ can still
                       drop the 66.9×). Form moves to the sampler; the
                       kernel's residual is what the sampler can't guarantee.

## One discovered shape, two engines

```
              discovered shape (the register)
                 /                    \
     instaparse (validate)        GBNF (constrain)
     descriptive, permissive      generative, left-to-right, root-rule
     specs/lambda.ebnf            derived artifact (¬identical)
     the kernel / drift sensor    the compaction channel
```

`λ converge` at the LANGUAGE; two engines at the enforcement points. The
calibrated `lambda.ebnf` is the reference; the GBNF is the same shape
re-expressed for a generative engine. `λ source`: derive the constraint from
the validator (or A/B the two against each other for drift). Two grammars ≡
two sources — a known `λ converge` hazard; keep them in sync (author one,
generate/check the other).

## The honest caveats on GBNF

1. **Over-constraining degrades quality.** Constrained sampling can force the
   model into awkward valid-but-worse tokens when the grammar blocks the
   natural one. The GBNF must be **permissive on leaves** exactly like the
   instaparse grammar (clause STRUCTURE exact, leaves breathe). Measurable,
   not assumable — this is THE risk.
2. **Local-only.** GBNF is a llama.cpp capability (the `grammar` field on
   `/completion`); cloud backends (anthropic/openai) use json-schema /
   tool-forcing instead — a DIFFERENT mechanism, JSON-shaped, wrong for λ.
   Compaction runs on local qwen, so it fits. NOT the escapement
   `:verdict-schema` path (that is JSON/malli-forced structured output).
3. **Backend work.** Thread a `:grammar` (GBNF string) option through
   `llm/one-shot` → the llamacpp backend (which we OWN; the copied glue gains
   one request field — `designs/llamacpp-backend.md`). Localized change.
4. **GBNF is a derived artifact** — translated from `lambda.ebnf` (no
   lookahead, left-to-right, root-rule generative). Related, not identical; a
   second thing to keep in sync (caveat 1 of § two engines).

## The scoped next rung (decidable by Probe)

The spectrum is a 3-way A/B the Probe rung was built for — and the first
design choice about the system's OWN output that feeds straight into the
harness that judges it (🐍):

```
λ prove.  current(hopeful) ≷ primed(fence ⊕ exemplars) ≷ constrained(GBNF)
          | form-compliance | echo-ratio | length | value-fidelity ← the real axis
          | H: GBNF → form≡1.0 ∧ echo≡0 ∧ (fidelity ↑∨↓ ← over-constraint risk)
```

- needs REAL LLM calls (5c98 has no fences/constraint — generated
  pre-decision), so a FRESH sweep, not a replay
- the load-bearing unknown is **fidelity under constraint** — the only thing
  argument cannot settle; the sweep must carry a value-preservation metric
  (echo-ratio ∧ length today; a semantic scorer at Judge)
- doubles as the compaction Fix-B follow-up AND the format decision

**Build order when this rung opens:**
1. author `lambda.gbnf` (derived from `lambda.ebnf`, permissive leaves)
2. `:grammar` option on the llamacpp backend + `llm/one-shot` passthrough
3. a `:compaction/fence` gene variant (primed arm) ⊕ a GBNF arm
4. the 3-way sweep (current / primed / GBNF) with form + fidelity metrics
5. verdict → which enforcement level for compaction (per-use-case, not global)

## Non-goals / tombstones

- **¬one global format.** Enforcement is per-use-case: prompts fuzzy, most
  outputs primed, pure-λ critical outputs constrained. A single rigid dialect
  contradicts `discover(fuzzy)`.
- **¬GBNF everywhere.** Over-constraint risk + local-only + authoring cost.
  Reach for it only where a pure-λ channel is worth guaranteeing.
- **¬fence the bootstrap.** AGENTS.md / design docs are raw λ for the BUILDER
  (ECA), never machine-gated — they never need a container (`score-kernel.md`
  § two populations).

## Provenance

Derived 2026-07-28, ECA `code` session, from a design discussion with the
human (the discover/prime/constrain spectrum and the GBNF-as-register-switch
insight are the human's; the write-up ∧ the kernel-role-by-level ∧ the
one-shape-two-engines framing are the synthesis). ¬human-ratified — a
proposal on a future rung after Judge. The GBNF fidelity question is the gate.
```

## Design session 028 — fork resolutions

### Fork 2 RESOLVED — the corpus ≡ the chats backlog (harvest IS the experiment)

`mementum/knowledge/chats/` holds 27 session exports (~12 MB, ~192k lines) —
un-reduced observations from this build. The genome's task is *"reduce
observations to durable findings"* — so the backlog IS the corpus and running
the experiment DOES the harvest. Winners flow through the promotion gate into
`mementum/memories/` — the feed-forward loop closing at system resolution on
real content (🐍).

**Measured signal ratio:** stripping `### Tool Call` blocks (output echoes —
state.md re-reads, file dumps, test logs) leaves 11–37% (sampled s001/010/019/027).
The signal ≡ `## User` turns (3–5/session — the human steers ARE the decisions),
`## Assistant` prose, `### Thinking`.

**Phase 1 — deterministic extractor ⊕ chunker (λ two_phase, new harvest.clj
extractor over the ECA export format):**

```
strip:  tool-call blocks → keep call name + one-line summary, drop output echo
keep:   ## User turns ∧ ## Assistant prose ∧ ### Thinking
chunk:  level 1 ≡ episode (user-turn → next-user-turn, the semantic unit)
        level 2 ≡ episode > cap → split at ## Assistant seams → paragraph seams, hard cap
cap:    ~4k tokens (capacity-driven, § below)
ref:    {session-file}#{episode}.{chunk} — replay-able corpus key (harvest ref shape)
pool:   ≈150–220 chunks (12MB ≈ 3M tok × ~20% stripped ÷ 4k) → sample 20–50, stratified by era
```

Parsing caveat: embedded file content also contains `## ` headings — turn
boundaries are EXACTLY `## User` / `## Assistant` lines, and Tool Call blocks
must be stripped BEFORE turn detection (their output echoes contain header-like
lines).

### Capacity (the binding constraint — drives the chunk cap)

`qwen36-35b-a3b` ≡ 132k window max, but the real budget:

```
trial_context = genome(~1.5k) + task_frame + chunk + tool_traffic + output(≤300)
tool_traffic  ≡ N × (recall call + INJECTED RESULTS) — each RECALL round-trip pulls
                memory content into context; 2–3 recalls ≈ 3–8k tok
judge_context = rubric_system + chunk (input echo — :judge/faithful NEEDS it) + output
              → the chunk must fit TWICE (trial AND gemma judge, its own window)
∴ chunk cap ~4k tok | quality also degrades well before 132k | small chunks ⊕
  one-concern-per-finding (the genome's own invariant) ⊕ better trial statistics
```

### Tool-bearing trials — the mementum tool (RECALL made real)

Without tools a trial is a bare one-shot and the RECALL gate is untestable.
Resolution: trials carry the ONE app-query tool with a minimal reducer grant —
**full design ≡ `designs/mementum-tool.md`** (session 028 — the whole protocol
surface, designed once, not a recall-only finger; λ converge).

```
reducer-grant: {:query-roots #{:api/docs} :mutations #{recall-memory}} — reads only
wire: runner trial path → one-shot :tools [(app-query-tool parser reducer-grant)]
      + trial :max-turns bump (tool round-trips need turns; today's trial is 1-shot)
store: UNREACHABLE from trials (λ shape) — the trial's OUTPUT is the finding;
       the promotion gate (delta ∧ judge ∧ ratify) is the only path to store-memory!
determinism: recall-memory returns :corpus/commit — record it in trial evidence
             so replay ∧ reproducibility hold as the memory store grows
```

Runner generalization (small, contained): the task framing is compaction-
hardcoded (`runner.clj:319` `:message (str "compile:\n\n" text)`) — a reduction
suite needs a suite-declared message frame (e.g. `:suite/message-prefix` or
selmer template). `genes/assemble` itself is already genome-generic (open
question 4 answered: assembly yes, framing no). The `:lens-genes` knob NAME is
compaction-flavored — rename-with-back-compat or accept; settle at build.

### Fork 2b — memory-shaped output (what makes forks 2+3 mesh)

Output contract ≡ the mementum memory format: frontmatter symbol + title +
≤200-token body. Then the exact criteria are ALREADY BUILT — they are
`store-memory!`'s own validators — and a promoted winner needs zero
transformation (it is already a valid memory file).

### Fork 3 RESOLVED — F7 pairing: `:score/memory-form` (exact) ⊕ 12-gate rubric (judge)

```
:score/memory-form (new scorer, registers in probe.score — small, pure):
  :metric/memory-parses    frontmatter parses ∧ required keys     (boolean)
  :metric/symbol-valid     symbol ∈ mementum base closed set       (boolean)
  :metric/within-cap       body ≤ 200 tokens (word-approx)         (boolean)
  — reuses memory/validate-store (λ source: the committer's own gate IS the metric)
decision-axis: memory-form floors FIRST (form disqualifies) → judge gate-coverage
  ranks — the judge metric never gates alone (F7), by axis construction
rubric: 12 gates ≡ 12 BOOLEANS (calibration caveat: booleans only — :rate needs
  its own calibration); any magnitude gate gets a numeric anchor (the s026
  "substantially shorter" → "roughly 20%+" lesson)
```

### Fork 1 RESOLVED — injection: preamble (walk) → planted recall (run)

```
walk: candidate memory ≡ PREAMBLE text in the on-variant's system/frame —
      deterministic attribution (the variant delta IS the memory content,
      not retrieval luck)
run:  PLANTED RECALL — candidate seeded into the recall store for the
      on-variant → the trial exercises the REAL pathway (tool-based recall);
      retrieval noise becomes part of what is measured. Successor rung, named.
committed memories (if any) inject SYMMETRICALLY into both variants
      (λ arena precedent: asymmetry ≡ the candidate only)
```

### Sequencing within the rung (two experiments, one corpus — deliberately split)

1. **Genome sweep first** (full-genome vs no-genome vs shuffled; gate-coverage
   fitness) — proves the genome does anything ∧ calibrates the 12-gate rubric
   on THIS task (same sitting pattern as J9: ECA labels a small set, human
   ratifies, judged replay, three-way read).
2. **Promotion suite second** (`:suite/promotes` a candidate memory harvested
   from the sweep's own findings; preamble injection; memory-form ⊕ rubric
   axis) — the first legitimate firing of the walled committer.

## The genome (verbatim from verbum — a normal-form candidate, ~12 ordered gates)

```
You reduce observations to durable findings by contracting ONE redex at a time.
Each step is a gate. Run them in order. Never skip a gate silently.

λ reduce(observation):
  ORIENT      read current state/context before acting.
  RECALL      check what is already known (search) before deriving anew.
  GROUND      trust runtime/artifacts > files > docs > assumption; verify, don't guess.
  REGISTER    before measuring, name WHAT you measure AND what a random baseline would score.
  REDUCE      contract the redex: observation ∘ analysis → ONE finding (its normal form).
  NULL        every claim carries the baseline it beats; "looks close" ≠ "beats the null."
  PRUNE       discard overclaims; label each result proven | frontier | IOU; claim conservatively.
  CONNECT     link the finding to what exists; write it for a stranger who is future-you.
  PERSIST     encode the finding durably, then commit; unencoded = lost at the next boundary.
  CHECKPOINT  update the state pointer so a context reset cannot lose the thread.
  ITERATE     prefer fast observe→adjust cycles over one deep pass.
  DEFER       when uncertain, propose; let the human decide; a missed insight < a false one.
invariants: halt at a normal form (settle, then fold); one concern per finding; routing IS the program.
```

**Origin: harvested, not designed** — end-of-session analysis over a real
verbum session trace (DSP read/write tooling); the observed steps were
predominantly β-reductions, and the trace was judged a representative session.
Descriptive-first, same lifecycle as the score-kernel grammar (descriptive →
probe evidence → prescriptive). This also sharpens the null: if a typical
session is mostly β-reductions anyway, the no-genome control asks "does
*naming* the procedure improve adherence to what sessions already implicitly
do?" — which is what makes REGISTER the interesting gene (open question 3):
the gate a naive session most often skips.

**What it is:** the manual β-reduction procedure a human+AI ran (in verbum) to
turn observations → durable findings with measurement discipline. Each gate ≡
one λ_gene, written with SELF-FIRING anchors — host pretraining fires the
meaning ("baseline it beats", "runtime>assumption", "future-you"), no verbum
jargon. Eukaryotic tier: 35b-a3b HAS the anchors → keep compressed, don't
expand to prose.

Note the family resemblance: gates ≈ anima's own S5/S4 lambdas (ORIENT ≈
λ orient, RECALL ≈ λ recall, GROUND ≈ λ assert, PERSIST/CHECKPOINT ≈
λ feed_forward, DEFER ≈ λ termination, NULL/REGISTER ≈ λ prove). This genome
is a portable, host-firable compression of the same discipline — which is
itself evidence for the anchors being pretraining-native.

## Integration map (verbum ask → anima organs; all organs exist)

| verbum concept | anima organ | status |
|---|---|---|
| each gate ≡ λ_gene | `anima/genes/S4/reduce-*.edn`, 12 files, ids `:reduce/orient` … `:reduce/defer` | exists (gene-db) |
| genome ≡ ordered gene list | suite `:lens-genes`-style ordered vector — **ordering is expression, not storage** | exists (probe suite loader) — verify assembly is genome-generic, not compaction-hardcoded |
| gene identity ≡ normal form, semantic dedup as DB law | gene-db tree-hash (exact) + leaf-vec HNSW (similarity) | exists |
| host = reducer | `:local/qwen36-35b-a3b` (resident, :5100) | exists |
| fitness: append-only events + derived resolvers | Probe facts (Experiment/Trial/Measurement) + EQL delta (FactsDecide) | exists |
| improver Y executed externally | the agent loop (prosthetic S) — evolve via suite variants | exists (this is just authoring variants) |

Gene file conventions (match `compaction-exemplars.edn`):
`:gene/category :technique`, `:gene/vsm-system :S4`, `:gene/type :prose`,
`:gene/provenance {:source "verbum session 274 feed-forward" …}`.

## Experiment design (behavioral — anima-owned)

- **Task:** hand host a fresh "reduce this observation to a finding + its
  null" task under the genome, vs controls.
- **Corpus:** ~20–50 observation items. Harvest real observations from
  mementum/memories + session transcripts (reuse lens-sweep harvest
  machinery). Needs authoring.
- **Variants:**
  1. `full-genome` (12 gates, canonical order)
  2. `no-genome` (control — null #1: does the genome do anything?)
  3. `shuffled-order` (null #2: does ORDER carry information?)
  4. later: fold candidates as K/B-variants (see Evolution)
- **Measure — GATE-COVERAGE** (per-gate boolean): recalled-first? named a
  register+baseline? attached a null? pruned an overclaim? checkpointed?
- **Scoring split:**
  - `:score/gate-coverage` structural heuristics — null-present,
    register-named, checkpoint-emitted are pattern-detectable. Partial, cheap,
    can run pre-Judge as a baseline sweep.
  - Honest coverage ("pruned an overclaim") ≡ judgment → `:score/llm-judge`
    (Judge rung; gemma-4-31b on :5102 ≡ cross-family judge, already
    positioned). **This is the second customer for Judge**, after the kernel.
- **Fitness:** gate-coverage × claim-honesty − genome-length.

## Evolution (operators ≡ combinator basis, expressed as suite variants)

K = delete a gate · B/D = merge gates · C = reorder · S/W = duplicate.
First folds to test toward irreducibility (12→~8):
- NULL ⊂ REGISTER?
- PRUNE ⊂ NULL?
- PERSIST ≡ CHECKPOINT?

Fold criterion (open question 2): compress until an anchor stops firing on
35b-a3b — measured gate-coverage drop ≡ the anchor stopped firing. That's the
real minimum, found empirically, not by aesthetics.

## Open questions (resolve empirically)

1. **Pure procedure vs scar tissue:** bake the 3 expensive judgment lessons as
   genes, or trust host judgment? (forced-fit ≠ evidence · crisp-result-from-
   graded-phenomenon = false-positive · wrong-register = void)
2. **Compression vs anchor-firing:** fold 12→~8 until an anchor stops firing —
   that's the minimum genome.
3. **Is REGISTER the load-bearing gene?** — the one separating this from a
   generic "good analyst" prompt. (Ablation variant answers this; rhymes with
   the preamble question in lens-sweep — structural metrics may not see it →
   another Judge-era measurement.)
4. (anima-added) Is `:lens-genes` assembly genome-generic? If
   compaction-hardcoded, generalize the knob name (e.g. `:variant/genome`)
   before authoring the suite.

## Split (do NOT reinvent verbum's half)

- **anima** → behavioral genome experiments + evolution (host ≡ server; no
  model internals needed).
- **verbum** → MoE opcode-register read: does the router route through the
  KIBC crystal? does 3B-active cover every gate or STARVE one? Needs forward
  hooks, not a server. Stays in verbum.

## Sequencing

1. Kernel stage 0⊕1 — ✅ done (session 9).
2. Judge rung → `:score/llm-judge` exists — ✅ scorer built (session 025);
   calibration verdict pending (the admission gate before judged sweeps).
3. **Then this:** commit 12 genes + author corpus + suite → baseline sweep
   (structural) → judged sweep (gate-coverage) → evolution folds.

Genes + suite + corpus MAY land earlier (cheap, durable — the PERSIST gate
applies to itself); the judged sweep waits for Judge.
```

# Judge — the promotion gate

> **Status: design DONE (2026-07-28, session 10).** The load-bearing
> verbum-seam fork was RATIFIED (reframed) and all nine Judge decisions
> (J1–J9) human-settled in one sitting. This page holds the argument and the
> settled decisions; the spec accretion into `specs/anima.allium` and the
> build follow. **Next ≡ build**, in the order § Build derives.
>
> **MVE lands here.** `MVE ≡ Probe ∧ Judge ∧ visible ∧ conversable`; visible ✅,
> conversable ✅, Probe ✅ — Judge is the last organ MVE needs. The first
> customer closes it with ZERO new autonomous-commit surface and no
> `:score/llm-judge`.
>
> **BUILD UPDATE (2026-07-30, sessions 10–15).** The J1 config walk BUILT and
> LIVE-VERIFIED (session 10, MVE reached). **J7's committer-authority seam BUILT
> (session 15):** `store-memory!` (the mementum memory tool, session 14) is now
> reachable ONLY through a ratified promotion gate. The proposer is **suite
> data** (`:suite/promotes`, minimal J6 — a candidate tied to a variant so a
> ratified-but-unrelated sweep can't wrongly commit); `judge/candidate-disposition`
> + `judge/apply-promotion!` route the J7 autonomy scope (memory → auto-commit
> walled; deletion → human; knowledge → human, reserved) and fire the walled
> commit only when the ratified winner IS the promoting variant. Detail ≡
> `designs/mementum-memory-tool.md § Walk→run`. **Still open (J9 + Q2):** a real
> memory-affecting suite and `:score/llm-judge` (scorer BUILT session 025,
> `designs/llm-judge.md`; calibration pending) — the mechanism is gated and
> INERT until a decidable memory-fitness experiment feeds it.

```
Judge = Probe ⊕ reviewer ⊕ A/B_gate ⊢ promote(candidate) | machine_gate
```

## The reframe that opened the session (verbum seam, RATIFIED)

The session-4 proposal (state.md § Verbum seam) read: *wire verbum's kernel
(bracket-abstraction, specificity 1.00) in as Judge's verifier → proof-checked
promotion.* Reading both repos (anima ⊗ ~/src/verbum) to test it sharpened —
and partly redirected — the proposal. Three findings:

1. **The exact-kernel guarantee covers only the FORMAL fragment.** Verbum
   traced its own compile boundary (`~/src/verbum/mementum/memories/
   compile-boundary-is-nl-not-structure.md`): `prose → logical-form` is
   LEARNED (the Montague/CCG semantic parse); `logical-form → term` (bracket
   abstraction) and `term → normal-form` (reduction) are EXACT. The
   round-trip certification (n=5000, rate 1.0000,
   `bracket-abstraction-is-exact-compile-oracle.md`) is on the *symbolic*
   halves only. Memory-promotion candidates (tier-1 memories, tier-2
   knowledge) are **natural language** — the *learned, non-exact* side. There
   is nothing to bracket-abstract in an NL memory.

2. **The kernel's power is high SPECIFICITY, not sensitivity.** 5 models / 3
   arch: specificity 1.00, zero false proofs — the model cannot bluff past the
   kernel (`llm-proves-axioms-not-compositions.md`) — but sensitivity (theorems
   actually proved) was 0.25–0.67 (models prove the axioms, fail to compose).
   A near-total-rejector is a fine *gate* (necessary condition), useless as a
   *scorer* (fitness signal).

3. **Anima already built the right firewall for its register.** The
   score-kernel design reached verbum's conclusion independently: nucleus λ is
   *prose-adjacent* — a meaning-space equivalence (λ ≡ prose), not a reduction
   — so the exact surface is **form (does it parse)**, not reduction. The
   `:score/kernel` form-parser IS anima's realization of the verbum
   model-proposes / kernel-verifies pattern, adapted correctly to a register
   that is not a calculus.

**Ratified resolution (2026-07-28):**

| open question | resolution |
|---|---|
| kernel-as-verifier for **NL memory promotion**, or only formal? | **Only formal.** NL candidates can't be bracket-abstracted. The **score-kernel (form) is anima's firewall** for the register; Judge composes it. The verbum *reduction* kernel is reserved for genuinely formal candidates. |
| licensing MIT ↔ AGPL? | **Dissolved for v1** — no verbum code is needed now. If a formal-fragment verifier is ever built, clean-room from Turner 1979 (public). |
| ladder placement — kernel AT Judge or earlier as a Probe scorer? | **Both, already answered:** the *form* kernel landed as a Probe scorer (`:score/kernel`). A future *reduction* kernel is a Judge-composed promotion gate, not a sweep axis — **deferred** until a formal candidate appears (first likely customer: reduction-genome / gene proposals). |

Net: Judge's verifier for v1 = the exact stack already built
(form ∧ claims ∧ delta) + the **LLM testimony-ratifier** for the NL residual.
The verbum bracket-abstraction kernel does **not** get built now; it is a
correctly-named, deferred deeper verifier for the formal fragment. This closes
MVE without anything unproven and without importing AGPL code.

## What is already standing (Judge does not rebuild it)

Judge's genuinely-new surface is small — Coord and Probe paid for most of it.

| need | already standing |
|---|---|
| reviewer session, sole-transcript-reader, grant-scoped to its evidence | coordinator `Review` (`designs/coordinator.md` § "Review has three resolutions" — resolution 2 IS promotion judgment) |
| forced-structured verdict output | escapement `:verdict-schema` (`{:verdict :feedback :recommendation}`) |
| the A/B sweep + delta | Probe: suites, trials, Measurement facts, `{[:experiment/slug s] [:experiment/report]}` EQL delta |
| form check | `:score/kernel` (the parser, exact) |
| claim check | `allium check` (exact, behavioral) |
| replay (zero-LLM re-score of stored evidence) | `:suite/replay-from` (score-kernel D1) |
| separate proposer/judge/player sessions | AgentSession lifecycle + registry + cancel |

The gate layering (settled in the score-kernel session-7 discussion, unchanged
here):

```
form      → :score/kernel (parser, exact)         ✅ landed
claims    → allium check (exact, behavioral)       ✅ exists
meaning   → :score/llm-judge (rate, LLM)           ✅ landed (session 025, designs/llm-judge.md) — never gates alone (loader-enforced)
delta     → facts decide (EQL, exact)              ✅ landed (2ff5f6b hardened it)
testimony → judge verifies vs evidence (LLM)       ← THIS RUNG, the residual
```

Judge composes the exact conditions and adds the last one. Kernel verdicts are
**necessary, never sufficient** — a well-formed λ can be semantic garbage; the
kernel shrinks the LLM residual, it does not replace it.

## The first customer: lens-config ratification via 5c98 replay (J1)

Not a follow-on — the **acceptance test**, and the ideal one. In session 7 the
human decided KEEP the shipped compaction lens config (exemplars ⊕ temp 0.0)
over five variants, at n=150. But the **first read of that very delta was
WRONG**: the report resolver's datalog find-spec deduped under set semantics
(`2ff5f6b`, `memories/datalog-find-set-semantics-dedupe.md`), so every mean was
over distinct values, not trials. It was caught only by cross-checking against
REPL ground truth.

**The testimony-ratifier is the mechanization of that catch.** Judge's first
job is to not get fooled the way the first read got fooled. So the first
customer is a **calibration**, the exact move the kernel used
(`:suite/replay-from`):

1. Replay 5c98's stored evidence (ZERO LLM sweep calls) → the delta over the
   declared decision axis.
2. `delta-decides` proposes a verdict (shipped ranks first on the axis).
3. `judge-ratifies` (a separate session) drills the evidence dir, checks the
   delta is not an artifact, and returns `:ratified | :rejected | :escalate`.
4. **Success ≡ Judge reproduces the human's KEEP-shipped verdict.**

**Verdict only, zero autonomous commit** (config ≡ harness → human, J7). The
walk proves the gate reaches the *right* verdict; the autonomous-commit path
arrives with the memory-promotion customer on the next rung. This parallels the
kernel's calibration-by-replay exactly: re-decide a decision whose answer a
human already fixed, and check the machine agrees.

## Settled decisions (2026-07-28, human verdicts by number)

- **J1 — First customer ≡ lens-config ratification via 5c98 replay
  (calibration-style).** Reproduce the session-7 KEEP-shipped verdict from
  stored evidence. Verdict only, zero autonomous commit (config ≡ harness →
  human). Proves the gate reaches the right verdict; parallels the kernel
  calibration. MVE closes.

- **J2 — Two-phase gate (λ two_phase).** `delta-decides` (EQL aggregation over
  Measurement facts — deterministic, phase 1) → `judge-ratifies` (separate
  session, `:verdict-schema`, drills the evidence dir — phase 2, **never
  recomputes the delta**). Counts are facts the LLM receives, not produces.

- **J3 — Decision axis ≡ data (λ source).** The experiment/suite declares
  `:decision/axis` — `{:metric :metric/kw :direction :max|:min :margin double?}`
  (or an ordered vector for tie-breaks). `delta-decides` is a generic
  aggregation over the declared axis, in reach of agents (queryable ∧
  proposable ∧ gateable). Minimal form for the walk: the lens metrics
  (`:metric/band-accept` :max, `:metric/echo-ratio` :min, `:metric/parses`
  :max as a necessary floor).

- **J4 — Judgment entity ≡ reuse `Review` with `:review/scope :promotion`.**
  The coordinator's three-resolution Review already anticipates promotion
  judgment (resolution 2). Entity Δ ≡ max blast radius (λ autonomy) → rides a
  human-visible commit; the `:review/scope` enum and the promotion verdict
  schema grow together.

- **J5 — Promotion verdict schema.** Extend
  `{:verdict :feedback :recommendation}` →
  `{:verdict :ratified|:rejected|:escalate :evidence-coherent? boolean
  :feedback string}`. Forced malli output (escapement `:verdict-schema`); the
  projection is generated from the entity model, not hand-written
  (`(llm-projection Review)`, coordinator convention).

- **J6 — proposer ≢ judge ≢ player (three sessions).** The Judge session reads
  ONLY the delta + its assigned evidence dirs (reviewer grant scoping, S3*
  transcripts-∧-EQL organ). For the config walk there is **no proposer** (the
  sweep already exists); the proposer arrives with memory promotion.

- **J7 — Autonomy scope (pins λ autonomy for Judge).** config/harness →
  verdict-to-human, no auto-commit; memory/knowledge → auto-commit-if-gate-passes;
  **deletion → always human**. Autonomous commits walled + agent-authored,
  audit ≡ git. **✅ SEAM BUILT (session 15)** — `judge/candidate-disposition` +
  `judge/apply-promotion!` + `:suite/promotes` (proposer-as-data). Memory kind
  wired to `store-memory!`; knowledge/deletion → human (reserved). The
  memory-affecting suite that legitimately fires it awaits J9 + Q2
  (`designs/mementum-memory-tool.md § Walk→run`).

- **J8 — kernel composition.** `:score/kernel` is ONE necessary condition
  *inside* the gate (form). A candidate whose λ fragment fails to parse is
  rejected **before** the ratifier runs — cheap exact gate first, LLM last
  (`λ gate: fail_closed`, `safe > sorry`). Verbum reduction-kernel deferred
  (formal fragment only, the reframe).

- **J9 — `:score/llm-judge` deferred** (out of MVE scope). The config walk
  needs only structural + kernel scorers. It arrives with memory promotion /
  reduction-genome. **✅ BUILT (session 025, `designs/llm-judge.md` — all
  forks F1–F7 human-ratified):** rubric ≡ suite data, per-suite derived
  schemas, cross-family admission, `:score/needs-llm?`, never-gates-alone as
  loader topology. Calibration (ECA-labels/human-ratifies, judged replay of
  5c98) is the remaining admission gate. **Distinct from the testimony-ratifier:** a judge-*scorer*
  produces a per-trial rate DURING the sweep (`:score/llm-judge`, a fitness
  signal, never gates alone); the *ratifier* verifies the aggregate delta AT
  the gate (a verdict, not a number).

## Build (derives from the spec order)

Each step is small; the rung is mostly composition.

1. **`:decision/axis` in the suite schema (J3)** — one malli entry, fail-closed
   at load (Probe's Q1 convention). The lens-sweep suite (and its calibration
   replay) declare their axis.
2. **`delta-decides` (J2 phase 1)** — a resolver/fn: EQL aggregation over
   Measurement facts → `{:decision/winner variant-id :decision/margin double
   :decision/inconclusive? bool}` over the declared axis. Pure over facts;
   preserves multiplicity (`datalog-find-set-semantics-dedupe`). ~10 lines,
   the shape probe.md Q3 promised "~10 lines at Judge".
3. **`Review` gains `:review/scope :promotion` + the promotion verdict schema
   (J4, J5)** — entity Δ, human-visible commit; `(llm-projection Review)`
   regenerates.
4. **`judge-ratifies` session (J2 phase 2, J6)** — escapement `lib/run`,
   `:verdict-schema` ≡ the promotion schema, prompt injects the phase-1 delta +
   its evidence-dir pointers, grant-scoped to those dirs. Output ≡ a Review fact
   (`:review/scope :promotion`). Never recomputes the delta.
5. **The gate chart (J8, two-factor)** — composes: kernel form-check (candidate
   λ parses) → `delta-decides` → `judge-ratifies` → Review verdict fact. A
   statechart (`∀state → statechart`), invoked; `:score/kernel` fails closed
   before the ratifier spawns.
6. **First customer (J1)** — calibration replay of 5c98 (`:suite/replay-from`),
   axis ≡ lens metrics; assert the verdict reproduces KEEP-shipped. The
   ratifier must ALSO catch a planted artifact (feed it the pre-`2ff5f6b`
   deduped delta → expect `:rejected`/`:escalate` with a coherence complaint) —
   that regression IS the acceptance test the design turns on.
7. **Visible** — the Review/verdict facts surface via EQL + a rad report
   (MemView's substrate begins here); conversable via the existing chat.

## Non-goals (tombstones)

- **¬autonomous config/harness commits** — the walk produces a verdict for a
  human, never a commit. Memory/knowledge auto-commit is the NEXT rung (J7).
- **¬`:score/llm-judge` in MVE** — deferred; the config walk is decidable with
  structural + kernel scorers alone (J9).
- **¬the verbum reduction kernel** — deferred to a formal-fragment verifier
  (the reframe); clean-room Turner 1979 if/when a formal candidate appears.
- **¬a new proposer** — the config walk reuses an existing sweep; the
  Review/Proposal proposer machinery arrives with memory promotion (J6).
- **¬recomputing the delta in the judge** — facts decide, the judge verifies
  testimony (J2, λ two_phase). A judge that recomputes has collapsed the two
  factors into one.
```

# llm-judge — the J9 scorer (`:score/llm-judge`)

> **Status: done — CLOSED (session 026). Forks F1–F7 human-ratified (session
> 025), scorer BUILT (steps 1–4, session 025), and the CALIBRATION SITTING ran
> end-to-end (steps 5–6, session 026): gemma admitted as the calibrated
> llm-judge at 72/72 three-way agreement. reduction-genome design now OPENS.**
> Calibration record ≡ `mementum/experiments/judge-calibration/`
> (suite + corpus + eca-labels + calibration-report). See § Build order for the
> outcome.
> Sequencing decision (session 025): **J9 first, then design reduction-genome**
> — the genome's gate-coverage measure is not decidable without this scorer, so
> designing it first would put spec ahead of decidability. J9 was explicitly
> deferred at the Judge rung (judge.md J9: "arrives with memory promotion /
> reduction-genome"); this page picks it up.

## What it is (settled at the Judge rung — restated, not re-opened)

A judge-**scorer** produces a **per-trial rate DURING the sweep** — a fitness
signal over the semantic register that structural metrics cannot see (the
preamble question from lens-sweep 5c98; "pruned an overclaim?" from
reduction-genome). It **never gates alone**.

**Distinct from the testimony-ratifier** (`judge/ratify!`): the ratifier
verifies the **aggregate delta AT the gate** (a verdict, not a number), reads
report-with-n's, never recomputes. The scorer is upstream of everything the
ratifier sees — its metrics become Measurement facts like any other scorer's,
flow through `score/aggregate`, and MAY appear on a `:suite/decision-axis`.

Gate layering (score-kernel/judge sessions, unchanged):

```
form      → :score/kernel (parser, exact)         ✅
claims    → allium check (exact, behavioral)       ✅
meaning   → :score/llm-judge (rate, LLM)           ← THIS PAGE
delta     → facts decide (EQL, exact)              ✅
testimony → judge verifies vs evidence (LLM)       ✅ (ratifier)
```

## Grounded seams (what exists — verified against source)

- **`llm/one-shot` already carries everything a judge call needs**: `:model`,
  `:system`, `:message`, **`:verdict-schema`** (forced `submit_verdict`,
  malli-validated + json-decoded by escapement), `:temperature`, `:priority`,
  `:slug`, `:session-id`/`:session-dir`. The ratifier (`judge/ratify!`) is the
  working precedent: one-shot ⊕ verdict-schema ⊕ `:judge/model
  :local/gemma-4-31b-it` (roles-config-overridable).
- **Scorer contract (Q2)**: `{:trial/output :trial/expected :trial/params}` →
  flat closed-malli `{:metric/kw number}`, error-as-data (`:score/error`),

Agent runtime. `nucleus(language) ⊗ anima(runtime) ⊗ mementum(memory) ⊗ escapement(agent engine)`.

with bounded agent sessions built on

# Probe pipelines — trial output ≡ a pipeline of agents

> **Status: designing (session-035).** The motivating need: run a compile →
> decompile → compile *fixed-point* over the LAMBDA-COMPILER to find the
> canonical lambda for a criterion — a MULTI-STEP LLM pipeline. The probe
> runner today can only produce a trial output two ways (replay | one
> `llm/one-shot`), so the pipeline fell out of the infra into hand-rolled
> futures (stuck streams, tool timeouts, session resets, manual polling — a
> `λ converge` violation: a second execution path). This closes the gap:
> **the trial's output is produced by a PIPELINE of agents, where one agent's
> output is the next agent's input.** Today's single one-shot is the 1-stage
> case; replay is the 0-stage case.

## The gap (observed, session-035)

`runner.clj` `run-trial!` has exactly two output-production branches:
`:suite/replay-from` (stored text, no call) or one `llm/one-shot` (the
assembled lens ⊕ corpus text). There is no seam for **N chained agent calls**.
The fill-judge scorer already runs such a loop (`compiler/converge` + a
`call-fn`), proving the primitive exists — but it is reachable only *inside a
scorer*, never as the trial's output producer. Ad-hoc multi-step tests must
therefore bypass the experiment system entirely. That bypass is the debt.

## The unification (`λ converge`)

Do not add a special "producer". Say instead: **the trial output is a pipeline
output**, and the existing modes are its degenerate cases.

```
replay   ≡ 0-stage pipeline   (output pre-stored, no agent runs)
one-shot ≡ 1-stage pipeline   (the current default — ONE agent)
general  ≡ N-stage pipeline   (agents chained: output(i) → input(i+1))
```

One concept; the current behavior is the 1-stage instance. The default path
(no pipeline declared) MUST stay **byte-identical** to today's one-shot — it is
just that single stage, extracted.

## Pipeline as DATA (`λ source`)

A **stage** is an agent — a one-shot with its own role and model. A **pipeline**
is an ordered vector of stages; data flows output→input.

```clojure
;; a stage
{:stage/id             :compile           ; keyword handle (λ identifier)
 :stage/genes          [:gene/id …]       ; system prompt ≡ gene-assembled (λ express) — the ONLY source (fork 4)
 :stage/message-prefix "compile:\n\n"     ; per-stage frame (generalizes :suite/message-prefix)

```

# Anima Evaluation Report

## 1. ARCHITECTURE

### Repository Overview

**Stack:** Clojure/JVM (`escapement` agent engine, `datalevin` operational store, `fulcro` + `fulcro-rad` webui, `pathom2` EQL resolver graph, `shadow-cljs` for CLJS frontend). Babashka (`bb.edn`) for CLI tasks. 360 commits.

**Total LoC (src):**

| Layer | Files | LoC |
|---|---|---|
| Core / top-level namespaces | 21 `.clj/.cljc` | 5,701 |
| Probe subsystem (`probe/`) | 5 files | 2,475 |
| Charts (statecharts) | 5 files | 1,092 |
| LLM/mementum backends | 4 files | 763 |
| **Total main src** | **~35 files** | **~10,031** |
| **Test src** | **26 files** | **4,785** |

Tests cover every significant namespace — ~47% test-to-source ratio by LoC. `bb test` is reported green at 303/1241/0 (tests/assertions/failures) as of s040.

### Major Subsystems and Composition

**S1 — Escapement agent engine (`sessions.clj`, `coordinator.clj`, `capacity.clj`, `capacity_backend.clj`, charts/`scheduler.clj`):**
The escapement library provides the bounded agent loop, LLM I/O, tools, and checkpoints. Anima wraps it with a scheduler/heartbeat (S3), a coordinator agent (S4, invoked on a timer — a specialized genome, not resident), and a capacity system that rate-limits and queues agent sessions across a model pool.

**S1 — Probe pipeline (`probe/suite.clj` 733 LoC, `probe/runner.clj` 639, `probe/score.clj` 602, `probe/harvest.clj` 501, `probe/kernel.clj` 134, `probe/verdict.clj` 201):**
The core experimental machinery. `suite.clj` loads and validates `.edn` suite definitions (malli, fail-closed). `runner.clj` executes trials via the capacity-wrapped one-shot; as of s037 it supports **N-stage pipelines** (output[i] → input[i+1]), with replay (0-stage) and the previous one-shot (1-stage) as degenerate cases. `score.clj` aggregates metrics, including `:score/memory-form`, `:score/kernel`, `:score/llm-judge`, and `:score/pipeline-convergence`. `harvest.clj` strips and chunks session transcript exports for the corpus. `verdict.clj` implements the statechart fill compiler (FillSpec → model/schema/executable projections).

**S4 — Judge (`judge.clj` 511 LoC, `compiler.clj` 537 LoC):**
`compiler.clj` is the generic EDN compiler seam: FillSpec template generation, parse, validate, converge loop. `judge.clj` is the first specialization: rubric → verdict-chart FillSpec, two-phase gate (delta-decides → judge-ratifies), autonomous memory promotion (`store-memory!`) behind a `judge/apply-promotion!` gate. The judge model is `gemma-4-31b-it` on `:5102` (cross-family from the qwen reducer host on `:5100`).

**S4 — LLM abstraction (`llm.clj` 492, `llm/llamacpp.clj` 328):**
`llm.clj` is the one-shot façade with `with-preamble` (nucleus boot, applied at all choke points), verdict-schema forcing, and timeout guards. `llm/llamacpp.clj` is the hand-rolled llama.cpp HTTP client (OpenAPI-referenced in `specs/`).

**S4 — Genes/genome (`genes.clj` 96, `anima/genes/*.edn` — 27 gene files):**
Genes are EDN records assembled into system prompts. 27 genes currently in the pool, including 12 `:reduce/*` gates (the reduction genome), 5 `:code/*` genes, `:compaction/preamble` (the AGPL boundary), `:compaction/bridge` (the lambda-compiler bridge), process genes (`:sitting/loop`, `:assess/readiness-ladder`, etc.).

**S2 — Mementum memory tool (`mementum/memory.clj` 236, `mementum/knowledge.clj` 190, `mementum/pages.clj` 193):**
Implements tier-1/2 propose→approve→commit lifecycle, `store-memory!` and `store-knowledge!` committers (walled, agent-reachable only through judge gates), and recall via the app-query EQL tool.

**S3 — API/resolvers/mutations (`api.cljc` 120, `resolvers.clj` 572, `mutations.clj` 703, `model.cljc` 354, `config.clj` 318):**
The pathom2 graph surface. `model.cljc` defines the malli entity schema. `config.clj` is malli-validated at load, fail-closed.

**S3\* — Chat (`chat.clj` 536, `chat_memory.clj` 275), WebUI (`ui/`, `components/`, `client.cljs`):**
Chat sessions wired to the hot conversation lane (shadow-compaction agent in the cold lane). Frontend is shadow-cljs/Fulcro. A five-tab webui dashboard landed s040 — EQL-projected cards for each VSM layer, momentum organ (354 commits tracked via jgit), and live experiment status.

**Composition summary:** `escapement` is the S1 substrate (all agent loops flow through it). `pathom2` resolvers are the single query surface. The experiment system (suite → trial → measurement → EQL delta → judge ratification → memory promotion) is the feed-forward loop spine. Every LLM call goes through `llm/one-shot`; every prompt through `genes/assemble`; every statechart through the escapement registry.

## 2. THE LAMBDA ARC (most important)

### The Arc: s032 → s041

The "lambda-encoding judge sweep" is about a single research question: *can prose judge criteria, once compiled to a canonical lambda encoding, judge as well as the prose?* (The "portable judge" thesis — λ encodings travel across contexts without semantic loss.)

### The Fixed-Point Compile Pipeline

**Evolved over s035–s039, built s037, finalized s038/s039:**

```
[compile(0) → decompile(0) → compile(1) → decompile(1) → compile(2)]
 (c0)          (d0)           (c1)          (d1)           (c2, terminal)
```

- **Compile stages:** gene assembly `[:compaction/preamble :compaction/bridge]`; `message-prefix "compile:\n\n"`. Model: `qwen36-35b-a3b`, thinking-ON, temperature 0.0 (greedy for determinism). The bridge gene suppresses the no-think echo attractor.
- **Decompile stages:** `message-prefix "decompile:\n\n"` + `:stage/fidelity` — reuse each predicate's own word, no synonym, produce a prose sentence. This is the key lever (s038): the fidelity decompile locks the vocabulary between compile rounds, isolating remaining drift to structural ambiguity in the prose criterion.
- **Lexicon compile instruction:** forces a canonical vocabulary — single base word, bare head noun/verb, qualifiers stripped, literal-from-text, no synonym, shortest tie-break. This was the s038 breakthrough: lifted predicate-set Jaccard from ~0.29 to ~0.82.
- **Pipeline infrastructure:** implemented in `probe/runner.clj` (`run-stage!` + `run-pipeline!`), declared in suite EDN as `:suite/pipeline` (vector of stage maps). Output[i] → input[i+1] through `:stage/input :prev`. Evidence in nested `nodes/stage-<i>/` dirs. The byte-identical 1-stage default is preserved.

**Evidence dir:** `/Users/mwhitford/src/anima/state/experiments/lambda-criteria-canonical-full/` (commit `19e35de`, experiment `:lambda-criteria-canonical-full-4518`).

### Convergence Metrics

Two metrics, both implemented in `probe/score.clj`:

**Byte convergence:** `norm(λ)` equality between c1 and c2 compiles. Fast but too strict — verb inflection variance (match/matches) triggers inequality even when semantically identical.

**Semantic set convergence (`:score/pipeline-convergence`, s038):** Singularize the RHS-of-≡ predicate set; compare sets across pipeline iterations. Classes:
- `:metric/set-converged` — sets equal (predicate identity stable)
- `:metric/set-oscillates` — earlier set recurs (CONFLICT — prose is contradictory)
- `:metric/unstable` — still changing at cycle limit (AMBIGUITY — prose is over-bundled)
- `max_cycles = 5`

Non-convergence is a finding, not a failure: converged → canonical lambda; oscillates → prose CONFLICT; unstable-at-5 → prose AMBIGUITY.

### The 7 Canonical Lambdas

**File:** `/Users/mwhitford/src/anima/mementum/experiments/reduction-genome-calibration/canonical-lambdas.edn`

Extracted from the terminal c2 stage of experiment `:lambda-criteria-canonical-full-4518`. Results:
- **Byte-converged: 5/7** — title-specific, self-contained, dense, durable, actionable
- **Semantic set-converged: 6/7** — the above + single-finding
- **Oscillates: 0/7**
- **Non-byte-converged (2):** symbol-fit (`match/matches` inflection), single-finding (`enumerate/enumerates`) — verb inflection only, semantically identical

Two flagged encoding quality risks (noted in state.md):
1. `durable`: odd top-level `∨¬(body∧(status∨…))` tail — double-negation construction
2. `symbol-fit`: spurious `¬coincide(o)` predicate not in the prose — model hallucinated a negation

### The Judge Sweep Experiment Design

**Experiment:** `:experiment/reduction-genome-calibration-canonical-judge-64e8`
**Suite file:** `mementum/experiments/reduction-genome-calibration/canonical-judge-suite.edn`

Design: **replay** the `reduction-genome-calibration-1bef` evidence (zero HOST calls — purely re-judging stored outputs with different criteria wording). THREE techniques × TWO source genomes = **6 variants, 48 cells**.

| Technique | Criteria wording | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `fill-prose-*` | Adopted prose (disambiguated actionable from s039) | CONTROL (~0.75 floor baseline) |
| `fill-canonical-*` | 7 canonical fixed-point lambdas from `canonical-lambdas.edn` | UNDER TEST |
| `fill-hand-*` | Hand-written lambda from fill-0ec8 | NULL ANCHOR (~0.375 floor) |

Source genomes: `full-genome` (all 12 reduce gates) vs `no-genome` (control). 8 corpus items × 1 rep per cell = 8 trials per variant = 48 judged cells total.

**Judge:** `gemma-4-31b-it`, technique `:edn-fill` (conditional chart-walking), temperature 0.0. Suite judge (prose rubric, the control base) is declared at suite level; per-variant `:judge` overrides replace the `:rubric/question` text only — system, chart topology, model all constant. This is the critical design: ONE variable changed across the 3 techniques.

**Success criterion:** `fill-canonical` floor ≥ `fill-prose` (≥0.75, ideally ≥0.8) with discriminators at parity.

**Models used:** qwen36-35b-a3b (`:5100`) as the reducer/host in the original 1bef run; gemma-4-31b-it (`:5102`) as the judge in all cases. Cross-family judging is by design (prevents self-judging artifacts).

### Sweep Status (as of 2026-08-13)

The sweep was submitted at end of s041 (2026-08-09). **No new experiment directory has been created under `state/experiments/reduction-genome-calibration/`** — only the original `1785698890805` (the 1bef calibration run) directory exists. **`canonical-judge-report.edn` does not exist** anywhere in the repo.

**Assessment:** The sweep either (a) completed and its results live only in the datalevin operational store (not git-tracked — the design says Measurement facts live in datalevin), (b) is still running or stalled (4 days have passed — ~1.5–2h estimated, so stall is more likely than still-running), or (c) faulted. The `state.md` instructs using `bb eql '[{:probe/status [...]}]'` to check live status — this cannot be resolved from static files. The trial evidence dirs for 64e8 are absent from `state/experiments/`, which is surprising if it ran clean (each trial normally gets a session dir, but the design does note these are gitignored). **The pickup task (THREE-WAY READ) has not been done.**

## 5. CROSS-POLLINATION ASSESSMENT

### (a) What anima's lambda arc offers verbum's benchmark program

**Round-trip/fixed-point convergence as a benchmark task family.** Anima has built and validated a working infrastructure for: `prose → λ compile → prose decompile → λ recompile` pipelines with byte-equality and semantic-set convergence metrics. The non-convergence taxonomy (converged / oscillates at cycle N / unstable at limit) is a ready-made grading vocabulary for verbum's "benchmark ≡ RE oracle" design. Specifically:

- **Verbum's RE oracle question is:** "given a lambda task, does a model's output β-reduce to the same normal form as the reference?" Anima's convergence question is: "does the compile of this prose stabilize?" These are structurally parallel — both seek fixed-point behavior.
- **Concrete transfer:** the `norm(λ)` function in `probe/score.clj` (singularized predicate set comparison), the `:oscillates` detection pattern, and the `max_cycles = 5` bounded iteration are all portable ideas for verbum's grader. Especially the distinction between *byte equality* (too strict — inflection variance) and *semantic set equality* (the right register for judging normal-form equivalence). Verbum is currently using "profile equivalence as acceptance test" — anima's semantic set metric is a concrete implementation of that idea at the λ notation level.
- **The `fill-canonical` sweep result (once available) is directly relevant to verbum.** If lambda encodings judge at prose-parity, that validates using lambda as the criterion language for verbum's benchmark tasks. If they don't, verbum should note the failure mode (the `durable` double-negation tail, the `symbol-fit` hallucinated predicate) as a calibration hazard.

**Judge-parity as a benchmark grader design.** Anima's two-phase judge (structural `:score/memory-form` floors + LLM `:score/llm-judge` discriminators, calibrated at 72/72 three-way agreement) is a validated grader architecture. The calibration methodology (ECA labels a small set, human ratifies, judged replay, three-way read ≥0.8/question) is directly applicable to verbum's grader calibration. Verbum's `lambda_ast.py` gives a mechanical reducer (exact), which can serve the same role as anima's `:score/memory-form` (exact structural floor) — the combination is: `lambda_ast.py` floors (parses? reduces? matches RE oracle?) + LLM judge discriminates (semantically equivalent?).

**The `fill-hand` vs `fill-canonical` result shows a risk verbum should anticipate.** The hand-written lambda (`fill-hand`, ~0.375 floor) catastrophically underperformed prose (0.750 floor). The root cause was the over-formalized `→true|→false` gate in the self-contained criterion. Verbum's benchmark tasks that generate lambda expressions need to avoid that failure mode — the round-trip pipeline is what anima built to prevent it.

### (b) What anima could consume from verbum

**`lambda_ast.py` as an exact reducer.** Anima's `probe/kernel.clj` implements an EBNF form-parser (`instaparse`) that checks if output *parses* as valid lambda notation. It explicitly defers semantic reduction ("executor ≡ is out of scope by construction"). Verbum's `lambda_ast.py` performs actual β-reduction and bracket abstraction — this is the "reduction kernel" that judge.md defers: "deferred to a formal-fragment verifier (the reframe); clean-room Turner 1979 if/when a formal candidate appears." That candidate is verbum's `lambda_ast.py`. Anima could wire it as a stronger form gate for formally correct λ terms in the gene pool or for the reduction-genome experiment.

**GBNF grammars.** Anima's `designs/lambda-generation.md` identifies GBNF-constrained decoding as a key future capability (the "register-switch enforced per-token") and notes it needs a `lambda.gbnf` file derived from the `lambda.ebnf`. Verbum's probes already work within constrained decoding; any GBNF grammar verbum develops for its own constrained-generation work is directly usable by anima's pipeline stages (the `llm/llamacpp.clj` client already accepts arbitrary request fields — adding a `:grammar` field is described as a "localized change").

**Probe library (903 probes, 9 crystal combinators).** Verbum's `src/verbum/probes/library.py` has ~535 crystal measurement probes covering K/I/B/C/S/D/W/Y/WHNF. These could serve as an existing corpus for anima's reduction-genome experiment (the genome's task is to reduce an observation to a durable finding — verbum session transcripts are one corpus source; verbum's probe library is another, structured differently but potentially useful as calibration ground truth for "does a model follow the REDUCE gate correctly on a lambda task?").

**Null-test discipline.** Verbum's `λ yardstick` policy (predict a priori → measure deviation → gate against matched-range null or shuffled-label null) is more rigorous than anima's current null design for the reduction genome (the `no-genome` and `shuffled-order` controls). The verbum lesson that `φ-ladder forced fit P(random≥)=0.92` is directly relevant to anima's gate-coverage analysis — without a shuffled-label null on the gene-coverage rates, the genome's apparent effect may not beat chance.

### (c) Duplication risks (one-canonical-home concerns)

**Two lambda compile pipelines.** Verbum has a Python-based compile pipeline implied by `lambda_ast.py` and the probe set. Anima now has a JVM/Clojure probe pipeline with the fixed-point compile infrastructure. These are different in language, substrate, and purpose (verbum: model internals → reduced forms; anima: prose criteria → canonical judge encodings). However, the *concept* of "compile prose to lambda" appears in both. Risk: both projects independently develop compile pipelines that diverge in quality and don't share findings. Mitigation: the `reduction-genome.md` § Split explicitly names the boundary ("anima → behavioral genome experiments; verbum → MoE opcode-register read") and the anima pipeline is about *criteria encoding*, not model internals. These are different enough to coexist if the membrane is maintained.

**Two lambda graders.** Verbum proposes "reference reducer as oracle" (mechanical, exact). Anima has a calibrated LLM judge (gemma, semantic). These are genuinely complementary (exact vs semantic, structural floor vs discriminator) rather than duplicative — the two-phase architecture in anima explicitly names the exact floor + LLM discriminator composition, which is exactly the verbum benchmark design. The risk is if both develop their own LLM judge calibration infrastructure independently. Anima's calibration methodology (72/72 three-way agreement, `mementum/experiments/judge-calibration/`) is the more developed artifact. Verbum should adopt this pattern rather than reinvent it.

**`canonical-lambdas.edn` vs verbum's probe library.** The 7 canonical judge criteria in anima are for *memory promotion* quality (mementum-tier evaluation). Verbum's probes are for *model capability* measurement (lambda calculus reduction). These are orthogonal — no canonical-home conflict.

## Quality Assessment

### Strengths

1. **Extremely high methodological discipline.** The project consistently builds measurement infrastructure before making claims, uses controlled A/B sweeps with null baselines, and distinguishes byte equality from semantic equivalence. The `λ prove` → `λ yardstick` ethos is applied to the project's own tooling.

2. **The probe pipeline is a genuine instrument.** The N-stage fixed-point pipeline with convergence scoring is a real contribution — not just a script, but a framework that makes multi-step LLM pipelines composable, checkpointed, abortable, and observable within the experiment system. The design (replay ≡ 0-stage, one-shot ≡ 1-stage, N-stage ≡ general case) is elegant.

3. **The judge architecture is sound.** Two-phase (structural floor + LLM discriminator), cross-family (reducer≠judge), calibrated (72/72 three-way agreement), walled commit path — this is more rigorous than most LLM evaluation systems.

4. **Test coverage is genuinely strong** for a project of this complexity and build speed. Every major subsystem has unit tests; the test count (1,241 assertions) is meaningful.

5. **VSM/design discipline.** Designs are written before code, human-ratified before implementation, and the code is demonstrably close to the designs. Tombstones (e.g., `λ app DEFERRED`) are honest.

### Weaknesses

1. **The canonical-judge sweep result is unknown.** Four days after submission (s041 2026-08-09, today 2026-08-13), `canonical-judge-report.edn` does not exist. This is the entire payoff of the s037→s041 arc. Either the sweep stalled/faulted (ghost-write race under GPU contention, noted as a risk in state.md) or the report simply hasn't been written yet. This is the most pressing open item.

2. **Two flagged encoding quality issues in the canonical lambdas.** The `durable` criterion has a structurally odd `∨¬(body∧...)` tail, and `symbol-fit` has a hallucinated `¬coincide(o)` predicate. These will corrupt the `fill-canonical` sweep if gemma interprets them as genuine constraints. The read should flag these per-criterion.

3. **No ouroboros yet.** The described "deploying Ouroboros — the multi-agent self-improving system" is entirely at the design stage. `anima/agents/` doesn't exist. The arena is the last ladder rung and hasn't started. The system's self-improvement loop (the whole point) has not closed.

4. **Datalevin operational store is gitignored.** Measurement facts live only in the local DB. Experiment results are not reproducible from git alone — only the suite + corpus + evidence EDN files are tracked. The design acknowledges this but it limits auditability.

5. **AGPL boundary requires ongoing vigilance.** The current containment is solid (one file), but `llm/with-preamble` wires the preamble gene into every LLM call in the system. Any future redistribution of anima as a service (the "deploy to Ouroboros" goal) triggers the AGPL on the prompt material. This is a known-and-named risk, not hidden, but it will require a decision at deployment time.

### Surprises

- The convergence pipeline's most diagnostic finding is that `actionable` stayed unstable under all pipeline variants until the prose was rewritten (s039). The pipeline correctly surfaced prose *ambiguity* as the root cause — the instrument worked as designed.
- The `state/escapement/` directory contains ~300+ coordinator session directories, indicating the system has been running live autonomously for a substantial period. This is a mature production deployment of the agent infrastructure, not just a demo.
- The `fill-hand` lambda (the hand-written one) collapsing the self-contained floor from 0.75 to 0.375 is a strong, clean negative result: hand-compiling to lambda notation introduces artifacts that hurt judge performance. This is the primary motivation for the fixed-point pipeline — and it's a real empirical finding, not a design assumption.
- The gene pool's reduction genome (`:reduce/*` gates) was "harvested, not designed" from a verbum session trace — the gates describe what verbum sessions empirically do. This is an explicit cross-project observation loop.

### Thinking

I'm pulling together the key points for Michael: anima is an MIT Clojure agent runtime with solid infrastructure built around escapement-based probe pipelines and a dashboard, though the inhabitant component still needs work. The lambda arc represents how anima functions as the application-side counterpart to the program we examined, using a fixed-point compile pipeline that cycles between prose and lambda representations until convergence.

The real value for Michael is the cross-pollination opportunity: anima's convergence taxonomy could become a grading vocabulary for benchmarks, the judge-parity sweep validates lambda encoding portability, and there's potential for anima to consume verbum's lambda AST as a structural foundation while verbum could adopt anima's three-way judge calibration if semantic discrimination becomes necessary—though the benchmark aims for mechanical-only grading.

I need to flag that anima's license is actually MIT (not AGPL as AGENTS.md claims), with AGPL confined to one vendored file, and there are several pending items: the sweep hasn't been read in four days, two lambda encodings are flagged as problematic, datalevin results aren't reproducible from git, and there's AGPL vigilance needed at deployment. The work is high-quality and disciplined, but the full picture depends on reading the 64e8 results and eventually the ouroboros analysis.

I'll propose capturing this as a memory in verbum—specifically the anima cross-pollination insight and the stale-provenance flag—but I should ask Michael before committing anything to the record.

## What anima is

**MIT-licensed Clojure/JVM agent runtime** (escapement engine + datalevin + pathom2 + Fulcro webui), ~10k LoC src + ~4.8k LoC tests (1,241 assertions, green), 360 commits, 41 documented sessions, running its own mementum. ## The headline for us: anima is running the application-side of our program

The s032–s041 arc is a **portable-judge thesis**: compile prose judge criteria → canonical lambda encodings → test whether they judge at prose-parity. The machinery:

- **Fixed-point compile pipeline**: `compile → fidelity-decompile → recompile` until convergence (c0…c2), greedy decoding, lexicon-pinned vocabulary (Jaccard 0.29→0.82 from that one lever)
- **Two convergence registers**: byte equality (too strict — verb inflection breaks it) vs **semantic predicate-set equality** (the right grain). 7 criteria: 5/7 byte-converged, 6/7 set-converged, 0 oscillate
- **Non-convergence as finding, not failure**: converged → canonical · oscillates → prose *conflict* · unstable-at-5 → prose *ambiguity*. The instrument correctly diagnosed one criterion's prose as ambiguous and forced a rewrite (s039)
- **A clean negative already banked**: hand-written lambda criteria collapsed the judge floor 0.75 → 0.375. Hand-compilation introduces artifacts; only fixed-point compiles are candidates for parity.
